K K -theoretic wall-crossing formulas and multiple basic hypergeometric series
R. Ohkawa, J. Shiraishi

Abstract
We study K K -theoretic integrals over famed quiver moduli via wall-crossing phenomena. We study the chainsaw quiver varieties, and consider generating functions defined by two types of K K -theoretic classes. In particular, we focus on integrals over the handsaw quiver varieties of type A 1 A_{1} , and get functional equations for each of them. We also give explicit formula for these partition functions. In particular, we obtain geometric interpretation of transformation formulas for multiple basic hypergeometric series including the Kajihara transformation formula, and the one studied by Langer-Schlosser-Warnaar and Hallnäs-Langman-Noumi-Rosengren.



1
Introduction

1.1
Framed quiver moduli

Framed quiver moduli spaces

M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)

introduced by
King
[
21
]
and Reineke
[
41
]

are moduli spaces of framed quiver representations.
We can identify them with moduli spaces of
representations
of a quiver

(Q_{0},Q_{1})
with

a
set
Q_{2}
of relations and

a distinguished vertex denoted by
\infty\in Q_{0}
,

where
Q_{0}
is the
set of vertices, and
Q_{1}
is
the set of arrows
(see §
2.1
for
more detailed explanation).
We call such a collection of a quiver

(Q_{0},Q_{1})
with
Q_{2},\infty
a
framed quiver

Q
.
We set
I=Q_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}
.

For a generic stability
parameter
\zeta=(\zeta_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
sitting
inside the complement of union of hyperplanes called

walls
, we consider corresponding moduli spaces

M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
of

\zeta
-stable framed quiver representations where

\alpha=(\alpha_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
is a dimension vector of a fixed underlying
graded vector space of
Q
-representations.

For each vertex
v
of

Q_{0}
, we have a
tautological bundle

\mathcal{V}_{v}

over
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
, and
a natural homomorphism of vector bundles
\phi_{h}\colon\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}
corresponding to each arrow

h\in Q_{1}
.
More presicely, this means that the restriction

\{\phi_{h}|_{x}\colon\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}|_{x}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}|_{x}\}_{h\in Q_{1}}
to each point
x\in M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)

represents an isomorphism class
x
of
Q
-representations
.

We call arrows departing from
\infty

framings
, and
arrows arriving at
\infty

co-framings
.
The natural diagonal torus
\mathbb{T}
acts on moduli spaces

M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)

associated with
framings and co-framings.
Consequently, we can consider

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
K
-theory
over
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
.
To define
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant integrals over

M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
,
we add other types of
torus to
\mathbb{T}
if necessary so that
the affine algebro-geometric quotients

M^{0}_{Q}(\alpha)

have the fixed points sets

M^{0}_{Q}(\alpha)^{\mathbb{T}}=\text{pt}
, and
we have
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant morphisms
from
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
to
M^{0}_{Q}(\alpha)
.
For a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
K
-theoretic class
\Lambda

on
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(\alpha)
, we define the
K
-theoretic
Euler class
\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits(\wedge_{-t}\Lambda^{\vee})
,
where
\Lambda^{\vee}
is the dual of
\Lambda
,

\wedge_{-t}\Lambda^{\vee}
is the

t
-wedge product
\sum_{i\geq 0}(-t)^{i}\cdot\wedge^{i}\Lambda^{\vee}
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits
denotes the Chern character
(see §
3.4
for the precise definition).
When
t=1
, set
\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda)=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)|_{t=1}
.
We define
K
-theoretic integrals

\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)
in
§
3.4
.

We mainly study the case where
\Lambda
is
a linear combinations of
\mathcal{V}_{v}
and

\mathcal{V}_{v}^{\vee}
(
v\in Q_{0}
)
with the coefficients of
\mathbb{T}
-characters.
Then we write such a class by
\Lambda(\mathcal{V})
.

We introduce
t
-integer
[m]_{t}=(1-t^{m})/(1-t)
for a non-negative integer
m
,
and we set
\lvert\mathfrak{I}\rvert_{t}=[\lvert\mathfrak{I}\rvert]_{t}

for a finite set
\mathfrak{I}
.
We also set
[m]_{t}!=[1]_{t}\cdot[2]_{t}\cdots[m]_{t}
.

For two generic stability
parameters
\zeta^{+},\zeta^{-}

inside adjacent chambers along a wall,
we consider corresponding moduli spaces

M_{Q}^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha),M_{Q}^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)
of

\zeta^{\pm}
-stable
framed quiver representations.
A
wall-crossing formula
describes
the difference

\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)-\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)

for
\Lambda=\Lambda(\mathcal{V})
on

M_{Q}^{\zeta^{\pm}}(\alpha)
.
In the first part of the paper (until §3),
we give a framework
using enhanced master spaces
via Mochizuki’s method
[
26
]
,
and deduce the wall-crossing formula
in Theorem
3.4
.

There are many articles that use Mochizuki-style wall-crossing
(cf.
[
22
]
for
K
-theoretic
Donaldson-Thomas theory, and references therein).

To state our results, we need a choice
of a vector
\beta=(\beta_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
a vertex
\ast\in I
, and
a generic parameter
\bar{\zeta}

such that
0\leq\beta_{i}\leq\alpha_{i}
,

\beta_{\ast}\neq 0
, and

\bar{\zeta}
belongs to the
hyperplane
\beta^{\perp}
.
By the genericity assumption of
\bar{\zeta}
,
we have only two chambers whose boundaries contain
\bar{\zeta}

(see the beginning of
§
2.2
for
the presice choice of
\bar{\zeta}
).
Let
\mathcal{C}^{-}
denotes one of them
characterized by the condition
\sum_{i\in I}\zeta^{-}_{i}\beta_{i}<0
for any
\zeta^{-}=(\zeta^{-}_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathcal{C}^{-}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{+}
the other one.

Let
\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
denote the set
of collections
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})

(decomposition data)
such that

\bullet

\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)}

are disjoint non-empty subsets of
[\alpha_{\ast}]=\{1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{\ast}\}

\bullet

|\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}|=d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}
with
d^{(i)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
for
i=1,\ldots,j
, and

\bullet

\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)})>\cdots>\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})
.

We set
\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}=\{1,\ldots,\alpha_{\ast}\}\setminus\mathfrak{I}^{(1)}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\mathfrak{I}^{(j)}
,

\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})
and

\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert=d^{(1)}+\cdots+d^{(j)}
.

We take stability parameters

\zeta^{\pm}=(\zeta^{\pm}_{i})_{i\in I}
from the chambers
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}
introduced
as above.

Theorem 1.1
.

For
\Lambda=\Lambda(\mathcal{V})

on
M_{Q}^{\zeta^{\pm}}(\alpha)
,

we have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda(\mathcal{V}))-\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda(\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{u_{1}=0,\infty}\cdots\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{u_{j}=0,\infty}\int_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})

where
\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor
is a round down of the rational number

\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}
, and

\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})

is a localized

\mathbb{T}\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar_{k}}
-equivariant
cohomology class defined in
(
38
).
The integral
\int_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})
is a rational expression of

u_{1}=e^{\hbar_{1}},\ldots,u_{j}=e^{\hbar_{j}}

with the coefficients of the
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
parameters.
The symbol
\displaystyle\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{u=0,\infty}
denotes
taking a sum of residues at
u=0
and
u=\infty
.

We take
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant

K
-theory classes

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})

defined by a
linear combination
of
\mathcal{V}_{v}
and
\mathcal{V}_{v}^{\vee}

(
v\in Q_{0}
)
corresponding to

T^{\ast}M^{\zeta^{\pm}}_{Q}(\alpha)
on

M^{\zeta^{\pm}}_{Q}(\alpha)
.

Then we get a more concrete formula in Theorem
1.2

(Theorem
3.8
in the main
body of the text).

We introduce the Euler form

\displaystyle\chi(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})=\sum_{h\in Q_{1}}\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)}-\sum_{i\in I}\alpha^{\prime}_{i}\beta^{\prime}_{i}

(1)

for dimension vectors

\alpha^{\prime}=(\alpha^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}},\beta^{\prime}=(\beta^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
.

Theorem 1.2
.

For
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})
on

M_{Q}^{\zeta^{\pm}}(\alpha)
,
we have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})-\int^{[K]}_{[M_{Q}^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}^{\alpha_{\ast}}_{\beta_{\ast},j}\atop|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|=k}}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\prod_{i=1}^{j}{[d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1]_{t}!\over t-1}\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})

\displaystyle\cdot(t^{s(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(d^{(i)}\beta,\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta\right)}}-t^{s(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i},\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta,d^{(i)}\beta\right)}})\int_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha-k\beta)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),

where

d^{\leq i}=d^{(1)}+\cdots+d^{(i)}
for

\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})
,

\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}(\alpha_{\ast})=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
,

\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})
, and

s(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{I}^{\prime})

are defined in
(
44
),
and (
46
).

1.2
Chainsaw quiver variety

In the latter part of the paper, we study the chainsaw
quiver

where we index the vertices by
I=\{1,\ldots,N\}=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, and
take
r_{i}
framings and
r_{i+1}
co-framings
for each vertex
i\in I
.

The relation
B_{1}B_{2}-B_{2}B_{1}+zw=0
is
imposed at each vertex
i\in I
.
The associated framed quiver moduli spaces

M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
are called
the
chainsaw quiver variety

of type
A^{(1)}_{N-1}
where

\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{i})_{i\in I}
is the framing,
and
\boldsymbol{v}=(v_{i})_{i\in I}
is the dimension vector
of a fixed underlying graded vector space.

We take two types of
K
-theory classes
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=T^{\ast}M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
and

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}
(see (
72
) and (
76
)
as to the definitions in terms of virtual vector bundles
using tautological bundles).
We take two stability parameters

\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}=(-1,\ldots,-1),\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}=(1,\ldots,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
, and
call
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=M^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

the
affine Laumon space
of type
A^{(1)}_{N-1}
, and

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=M^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

the
dual
of
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
.
Notice that there may be many walls separating
\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}

and
\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}
in general.

We call the generating series

\displaystyle Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),

\displaystyle Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})

partition functions
, where
q,\kappa,t,\boldsymbol{e}=\left((e_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
,

\boldsymbol{\mu}=\left((\mu_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
, and

\boldsymbol{\nu}=\left((\nu_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}

are

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant parameters.
Remark that we have substituted
t=1

in the latter series
Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}
and

\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}
.

Originally, Nekrasov
[
30
]
introduced
similar partition functions for
N=1
and conjectured
that these partition functions give
deformations of the Seiberg-Witten prepotentials for

N=2
SUSY Yang-Mills theory.
This conjecture is proven in Braverman-Etingof
[
5
]
, Nekrasov-Okounkov
[
31
]

and Nakajima-Yoshioka
[
32
]
independently.

Combinatorial descriptions of
torus fixed points of framed moduli spaces may have
different appearance according to a choice of stability parameters.
Neverthless they sometimes have the same
torus equivariant integrals
as in Conjecture

1.3
(2) below.
This turns out to be the
case for integrals of the adjoint matter classes
over the Nakajima quiver varieties
(cf.
[
2
]
).
They are studied in
[
4
]
,
[
39
]
,
[
40
]
, and wall-crossing phenomena are
applied in different context from
the present paper.
On the other hand,
we study the
qq
-Painlvé
VI equation and its relationship with
the torus equivariant integrals over the
affine Laumon spaces in
[
6
]
,

[
7
]
.
It may be worth investigating this relation and possible representation
theoretic backgroud
in future.

We give some conjectures concerning relationships among
the dual partition functions for each type of

K
-theory classes.

We define several infinite products
(see
[
17
]
)

\displaystyle(x;q)_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=0}^{\infty}(1-xq^{l})

\displaystyle(x;q,t)_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\prod_{l,m=0}^{\infty}(1-xq^{l}t^{m})

\displaystyle(x;q,\kappa,t)_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\prod_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}(1-xq^{l}\kappa^{m}t^{n}).

Conjecture 1.3
.

(1)
For
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}=(\delta_{il})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
we have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}{(qp_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(tp_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(qtp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(\kappa^{N}tp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(t^{2}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(q\kappa^{N}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}},

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}{(qp_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(tp_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(qtp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(\kappa^{N}tp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(t^{2}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(q\kappa^{N}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}.

(2)
When
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=r
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa).

We describe behaviour under taking limits of
parameters
\boldsymbol{e}
in Proposition
4.10
,
from which we deduce functional equations
for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
from the Conjecture
1.3
.

Theorem 1.4
.

We assume Conjecture
1.3
.
Then for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)}=

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}\prod_{l\in I}{(qt^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{(t^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(qt^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

(2)

where
\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert=r_{1}+\cdots+r_{N}
.

In recent preprint
[
2
]
, Conjecture

1.3
is applied to study Vafa-Witten
invariants introduced by
[
43
]
and
[
44
]
.

For the fundamental matter class, we give functional
equations including “
q
to the minus Laplacian”

q^{-\Delta}
defined in (
88
).
This gives factorized form of
\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}/Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}
for
N=1,2
.

Conjecture 1.5
.

We have

\displaystyle\left({e_{1}e_{2}\cdots e_{N}p_{1}p_{2}\cdots p_{N}\over\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\cdots\nu_{N}};\kappa^{N}\right)_{\infty}\cdot\sum_{l_{1}\geq 0}\cdots\sum_{l_{N}\geq 0}{q^{(l_{1}l_{2}+l_{2}l_{3}+\cdots+l_{N}l_{1})/2}\over(q;q)_{l_{1}}(q;q)_{l_{2}}\cdots(q;q)_{l_{N}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(-q^{1/2}e_{2}p_{1}\over\nu_{2}\right)^{l_{1}}\left(-q^{1/2}e_{3}p_{2}\over\nu_{3}\right)^{l_{2}}\cdots\left(-q^{1/2}e_{1}p_{N}\over\nu_{1}\right)^{l_{N}}

\displaystyle\cdot q^{\sum_{i\in I}(l_{i+1}-l_{i-1})\vartheta_{i}/2}q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\cdots\mu_{N}p_{1}p_{2}\cdots p_{N}\over e_{1}e_{2}\cdots e_{N}};\kappa^{N}\right)_{\infty}\cdot\sum_{l_{1}\geq 0}\cdots\sum_{l_{N}\geq 0}{q^{(l_{1}l_{2}+l_{2}l_{3}+\cdots+l_{N}l_{1})/2}\over(q;q)_{l_{1}}(q;q)_{l_{2}}\cdots(q;q)_{l_{N}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{1}p_{1}\over e_{1}\right)^{l_{1}}\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{2}p_{2}\over e_{2}\right)^{l_{2}}\cdots\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{N}p_{N}\over e_{N}\right)^{l_{N}}

\displaystyle\cdot q^{\sum_{i\in I}(-l_{i+1}+l_{i-1})\vartheta_{i}/2}q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa),

where we use symbols
e_{i}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}e_{(i,\alpha)},\mu_{i}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\mu_{(i,\alpha)},\nu_{i}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\nu_{(i,\alpha)}

for
i=1,\ldots,N
, and

\vartheta_{i}=p_{i}\partial/\partial p_{i}

for
i\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
.

1.3
Handsaw quiver variety of type
A_{1}

We consider the case where
N=2
and
\boldsymbol{v}=(0,n)

to get the
Laumon space

M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},(0,n))
of type
A_{1}
, and the dual

M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},(0,n))
.

Here we
use
r_{0}=r_{2}
and
p=p_{2}
, and substitute

p_{1}=0
and
\kappa=1
.
Set
e_{\alpha}=e_{(0,\alpha)},\mu_{\alpha}=q/\mu_{(0,\alpha)}
for
\alpha=1,\ldots,r_{0}
,

e_{r_{0}+\alpha}=e_{(1,\alpha)}
,

\mu_{r_{0}+\alpha}=1/\nu_{(1,\alpha)}
for

\alpha=1,\ldots,r_{1}
, and

\displaystyle Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p^{n}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),

\displaystyle Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p^{n}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}).

Theorem 1.6
.

(a) We study
combinatorial description of torus fixed points, and
derive the explicit formula (in Theorem
5.3
)
for
Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\left(t^{r_{0}}p\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(tq^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{1}\\
\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\beta}/te_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

(3)

\displaystyle Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{0}}\end{subarray}}\left(t^{r_{1}}p\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(tq^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\alpha}/te_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

(4)

where
J_{0}=\{1,\ldots,r_{0}\}
and

J_{1}=\{r_{0}+1,\ldots,r_{0}+r_{1}\}
.

(b)
By the wall-crossing formula Theorem
3.8
,
we get functional equation
(in Theorem
5.4
)

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=\frac{(qt^{r_{0}}p;q,t)_{\infty}(t^{r_{1}+1}p;q,t)_{\infty}}{(t^{r_{0}+1}p;q,t)_{\infty}(qt^{r_{1}}p;q,t)_{\infty}}\cdot Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p),

(5)

where
(x;q,t)_{\infty}=\prod_{m,n=0}^{\infty}(1-xq^{n}t^{m})

is the double infinite product.

Some remarks are in order concerning our proof of the wall-crossing
formula (
5
) in Theorem

1.6
.
Recall that our argument uses enhanced master spaces via Mochizuki
method summarized in Theorem
3.8
.
One has an involved combinatorial structure
in Theorem
3.8

which prevents us from computing the coefficients
explicitly in general.
However when
r_{0}=r_{1}=m
, we can show the vanishing of the
coefficients (Lemma
6.2
).
Then from Theorem
3.8
,
Theorem
5.3
, and Lemma
6.2
we have that

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{(m,m)}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{(m,m)}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p).

(6)

This transformation formula (
6
) has been
already studied in the study of hypergeometric series
from various contexts.

Theorem 1.7

(Langer-Schlosser-Warnaar
[
25
]
, Hallnäs-Langmann-Noumi-Rosengren
[
19
]
)
.

By taking trigonometric limit of

[
25
, Cor. 4.3]
, or
[
19
, (6.7)]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{m}\end{subarray}}\left(q^{m}p/\tau^{m}\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{m}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}x_{\alpha}/\tau x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}x_{\alpha}/x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\frac{(x_{\beta}y_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qx_{\beta}y_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{m}\end{subarray}}\left(q^{m}p/\tau^{m}\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{m}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}y_{\alpha}/\tau y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}y_{\alpha}/y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\frac{(y_{\beta}x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qy_{\beta}x_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

(7)

Remark 1.8
.

One finds that the formula (
7
)
is identical to
the formula (
6
)
after substituting
\tau=q/t
,

x_{\alpha}=e_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in J_{1}
, and

y_{\alpha}=q/te_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in J_{0}

in (
3
) and (
4
).
Therefore our geometric proof obtained in this article
gives another interpretation
of (
7
).

A proof of (
5
) in the general case where

r_{0}\neq r_{1}
is given by the
following result due to M. Noumi (private communication).

Theorem 1.9

(Noumi)
.

From Theorem
1.7
, when
r_{0}\leq r_{1}

it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\left(q^{r_{0}}p/\tau^{r_{0}}\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}x_{\alpha}/\tau x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}x_{\alpha}/x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{1}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}\frac{(x_{\beta}y_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qx_{\beta}y_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{r_{1}-r_{0}}\frac{(q^{r_{0}+s}p/\tau^{r_{0}+s-1};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{r_{0}+s}p/\tau^{r_{0}+s};q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{0}}\end{subarray}}\left(q^{r_{1}}p/\tau^{r_{1}}\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r_{0}}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}y_{\alpha}/\tau y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}y_{\alpha}/y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{0}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{(y_{\beta}x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qy_{\beta}x_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

(8)

Proof.

We reproduce Noumi’s proof.
Set

F_{r_{1},r_{0}}(x,y;p)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}p^{|\boldsymbol{k}|}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}x_{\alpha}/\tau x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}x_{\alpha}/x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{1}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}\frac{(x_{\beta}y_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qx_{\beta}y_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

We need to compare

F_{r_{1},r_{0}}(x,y;p)
with
F_{r_{0},r_{1}}(y,x;p)
.
From Theorem
1.7
, it follows that

F_{m,m}(x,y;p)=F_{m,m}(y,x;p)
.

For general
r_{0},r_{1}
, we set
y^{\prime}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{r_{0}-1})

and take the limit
y_{r_{0}}\to 0
.
We have

\displaystyle\lim_{y_{r_{0}}\to 0}F_{r_{1},r_{0}}(x,y^{\prime},y_{r_{0}};p)=F_{r_{1},r_{0}-1}(x,y^{\prime};p).

On the other hand, we can arrange the factors as

\displaystyle F_{r_{0},r_{1}}(y^{\prime},y_{r_{0}},x;p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{0}}\end{subarray}}p^{|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}|}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r_{0}-1}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}y_{\alpha}/\tau y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}y_{\alpha}/y_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{0}-1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{(y_{\beta}x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qy_{\beta}x_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{\beta}}}\cdot p^{k_{r_{0}}}\frac{(q^{-k_{r_{0}}+1}/\tau;q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}{(q^{-k_{r_{0}}};q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}-1}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{r_{0}}+1}y_{\alpha}/\tau y_{r_{0}};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{r_{0}}}y_{\alpha}/y_{r_{0}};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\cdot\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{0}-1}\frac{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}y_{r_{0}}/\tau y_{\beta};q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}{\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}y_{r_{0}}/y_{\beta};q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}\cdot\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{(y_{r_{0}}x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}{(qy_{r_{0}}x_{\alpha}/\tau;q)_{k_{r_{0}}}},

where
\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{r_{0}-1})

for
\boldsymbol{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{r_{0}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{0}}
.
Hence we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{y_{r_{0}}\to 0}F_{r_{0},r_{1}}(y^{\prime},y_{r_{0}},x;p)

\displaystyle=F_{r_{0}-1,r_{1}}(y^{\prime},x;qp/\tau)\cdot\sum_{k_{r_{0}}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\tau;q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}{(q;q)_{k_{r_{0}}}}\left(\frac{qp}{\tau}\right)^{k_{r_{0}}}

\displaystyle=F_{r_{0}-1,r_{1}}(y^{\prime},x;qp/\tau)\cdot\frac{(qp;q)_{\infty}}{(qp/\tau;q)_{\infty}}.

For any
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
,
we start from
F_{r_{1},r_{1}}(x,y;p)=F_{r_{1},r_{1}}(y,x;p)

and get

\displaystyle F_{r_{1},r_{0}}(x,y;p)

\displaystyle=\lim_{y_{r_{0}+1}\to 0}\cdots\lim_{y_{r_{1}}\to 0}F_{r_{1},r_{1}}(y,y_{r_{0}+1},\ldots,y_{r_{1}},x;p)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{r_{1}-r_{0}}\frac{(q^{s}p/\tau^{s-1};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{s}p/\tau^{s};q)_{\infty}}\cdot F_{r_{0},r_{1}}(y,x;q^{r_{1}-r_{0}}p/\tau^{r_{1}-r_{0}}).

∎

To write the explicit formula for

Z^{\pm}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}
,
let
\mathcal{B}
be the
q
-Borel transformation acting on
p
-series
defined by

\mathcal{B}\cdot p^{n}=q^{n(n+1)/2}p^{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Then in Theorem
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{-1}Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{1}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(-p\prod_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\frac{q}{e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{1}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e})}{\Delta_{J_{1}}(\boldsymbol{e})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{1}\\
\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

(9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{-1}Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(p\prod_{\alpha\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{0}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}{\Delta_{J_{0}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

(10)

Theorem 1.10
.

Applying the wall-crossing formula Theorem
3.4
,
we get functional equations
(in Theorem
5.9
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{-1}Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)=\frac{(-e_{r_{0}+1}\mu_{r_{0}+1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r}p;q)_{\infty}}{(-q^{r_{0}}p/e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{-1}Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p).

(11)

We recall the Kajihara transformation
[
20
, (4.8)]

for multiple basic
hypergeometric series.
following the formulation in
[
18
]
.
Given four collections of variables

(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}),\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}),(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
, and

(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})
,
Kajihara and Noumi’s
[
23
]
multiple basic hypergeometric
series are

\displaystyle\phi^{m,n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}&b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}&c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}\end{array};p\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{m}}p^{|\boldsymbol{k}|}\frac{\Delta(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{x})}{\Delta(\boldsymbol{x})}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{m}\frac{\displaystyle(a_{\alpha}x_{\beta}/x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qx_{\beta}/x_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{m}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(x_{\beta}b_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(x_{\beta}c_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

(14)

By
[
20
, Theorem 1.1]
,
the following
Euler transformation formula
holds
for general parameters
a_{i},b_{k}
, and
c
:

\displaystyle\phi^{m,n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}&b_{1}y_{1},\ldots,b_{n}y_{n}\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}&cy_{1},\ldots,cy_{n}\end{array};u\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(abu/c^{n};q)_{\infty}}{(u:q)_{\infty}}\cdot\phi^{n,m}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}c/b_{1},\ldots,c/b_{n}&cx_{1}/a_{1},\ldots,cx_{m}/a_{m}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}&cx_{1},\ldots,cx_{m}\end{array};abu/c^{n}\right),

where
a=a_{1}\cdots a_{m}
and
b=b_{1}\cdots b_{n}
.
We substitute
m=r_{1},n=r_{0}
,
u=-p/b
,
c=1
, and

a_{\alpha}=e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha},x_{\alpha}=qe_{\alpha}
for

\alpha\in J_{1}
, and

b_{\alpha}=e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}/q,y_{\alpha}=1/e_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in J_{0}
.
Then we see that (
11
) and (
1.3
)
are identical.
Our proof using the wall-crossing formula is
independent from the one in
[
20
]
.
Recently, Yoshida
[
47
]
also deduce these transformation
formula from physics arguments.

This paper is organized as follows.
First we recall Mochizuki method to prove our main results.
In section 2, we introduce framed quiver moduli,
the enhanced master space, and describe decomposition
into
connected components of the fixed points set of the
enhanced master space.
In section 3, we present wall-crossing formula to compute
equivariant integrals over framed moduli recursively.
In section 4, we introduce the chainsaw quiver variety,
give combinatorial descriptions of the fixed points
sets, and write explicit formula, and state
Conjecture
4.6
and Conjecture
4.14

concerning relationships with the dual partition
functions.
Then in section 5, we introduce the handsaw quiver variety of type
A_{1}
,
and give an explicit formula.
In section 6, we evaluate residues in the wall-crossing
formula
in this setting, and give a proof of our main results
Theorem
5.4
and Theorem
5.9
.

2
Framed quiver moduli and enhanced master space

2.1
Setting

Let

(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be a quiver, i.e. a directed graph, where

Q_{0}
denotes the set of the vertices,

Q_{1}
the set of the arrows.
For each arrow
h\in Q_{1}
, denote by

\text{out}(h)/\penalty\text{in}(h)\in Q_{0}

the beginning
/\penalty
ending of
h
.
A path
p
is a composable sequence
h_{1}h_{2}\cdots h_{k}

of arrows satisfying
\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h_{i})=\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h_{i+1})

for
i=1,\ldots,k-1
.
For the path
p=h_{1}h_{2}\cdots h_{k}
, we set
\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(p)=\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h_{k})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(p)=\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h_{1})
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A(Q_{0},Q_{1})}
be the
\mathbb{C}
-vector space consisting of formal finite
\mathbb{C}
-linear combinations of paths.
An associative algebra structure on

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A(Q_{0},Q_{1})}
is induced by compositions of paths.
We call
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A(Q_{0},Q_{1})}

a path algebra.

We consider a factored algebra

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A(Q_{0},Q_{1})}/J
,
where
J
is
a two-sided ideal generated by
some relations.
To be more precise, a relation is defined to be an
element
\gamma=c_{1}p_{1}+\cdots+c_{m}p_{m}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A(Q_{0},Q_{1})}

satisfying

\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(p_{1})=\cdots=\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(p_{m})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(p_{1})=\cdots=\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(p_{m})
.
For such
\gamma
, we set
\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)=\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(p_{1})

and
\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)=\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(p_{1})
.
Let
Q_{2}=\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{s}\}
be a set of the relations.
Then set
J=\langle Q_{2}\rangle

as a two-sided ideal
.
A collection
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{0},Q_{1}},Q_{2})
is called a
quiver with
relations
.

For geometric constructions, we take a collection

({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{0},Q_{1}},Q_{2},\infty)

consisting of a quiver

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(Q_{0},Q_{1})}
,
relations
Q_{2}
,
and a distinguished vertex
\infty\in Q_{0}

called
a framing vertex
.
We set
I=Q_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}
and
assume that
\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma),\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)\in I
for any

\gamma\in Q_{2}
.
Such a collection
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1},Q_{2},\infty)}
is called a

framed quiver
.

Let
V=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}
be a
finite dimensional
Q_{0}
-graded vector space
with
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\infty}=1
, and set

\displaystyle\alpha=(\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}.

(19)

Let

\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}\nolimits_{Q}(V)=\{\rho\colon A(Q_{0},Q_{1})/\langle Q_{2}\rangle\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V)\mid\text{ algebra homomorphisms }\}

be the set of
Q
-representations on
V
.
This is a zero set of a

map

\mu
defined as follows.
We set

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{Q}=\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)=\prod_{h\in Q_{1}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}),\quad\mathbb{L}_{Q}=\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)=\prod_{\gamma\in R}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)}),

(20)

and define
\mu=\mu_{Q}\colon\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)\to\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)
by sending

B=(b_{h})_{h\in Q_{1}}\in\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)
to

\mu(B)=(b_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}
where

b_{\gamma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})
is defined from
B
first by
taking a composition along each path, and then making a
linear combination of the contributions from the paths,
according to
\gamma
.
We identify
\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}\nolimits_{Q}(V)
with
\mu^{-1}(0)
.

Recall that
I=Q_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}
.
For describing stability conditions, we need
(\zeta_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
and

\displaystyle\zeta_{\infty}=-\sum_{i\in I}\zeta_{i}\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{i}.

(21)

For a
Q_{0}
-graded subspace
S=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}S_{v}
of
V

with
S_{v}\subset V_{v}
, set

\zeta(S)=\sum_{v\in Q_{0}}\zeta_{v}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits S_{v}
.
Note that

\zeta(V)=0

always holds when
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\infty}=1
by (
21
).
In what follows, we only consider the case where a
Q_{0}
-graded vector space
V=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}
satisfies
the condition
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\infty}=1
.
Given
Q
-representation

B=(b_{h})_{h\in Q_{1}}

on
V
, when
b_{h}(S_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)})\subset S_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}
for any arrow
h
of
Q
, we say that
S
is a sub-representation of
B
.

Definition 2.1
.

A
Q
-representation
B
on
V
is said to be
\zeta
-semistable
if for any sub-representation
S
of
B
, we have
\zeta(S)\leq 0
.
Furthermore
B
is said to be
\zeta
-stable if
\zeta(S)<0
for any non-trivial
proper sub-representation
S
of
B
.

For any
Q_{0}
-graded sub-space
S
of
V
, we have
\zeta(S)+\zeta(V/S)=\zeta(V)=0
.
Hence we can replace
\zeta(S)\leq 0
( resp.
\zeta(S)<0
) with
\zeta(V/S)\geq 0
( resp.
\zeta(V/S)>0
)
in the above definition.

Set
\Delta(\alpha)=\{\beta=(\beta_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}\mid 0\leq\beta_{i}\leq\alpha_{i}=\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{i}\ \text{ for any }i\in I,\text{ and }\beta\neq(0)_{i\in I}\}
.
Then each element
\beta\in\Delta(\alpha)
defines a hyperplane

\beta^{\perp}=\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{I}\mid\sum_{i\in I}\zeta_{i}\beta_{i}=0\}

called a
wall
.
A connected component of
\mathbb{R}^{I}\setminus\bigcup_{\beta\in\Delta(\alpha)}\beta^{\perp}
is called
a
chamber
where stability and semi-stability in Definition
2.1
coincide.
When
\zeta
belongs to a single chamber, the sign of
\zeta(S)
does not change.
Hence
\zeta
-stability conditions for
\zeta
in a single chamber
\mathcal{C}
are all equivalent.
We sometimes say that a
Q
-representation
\rho
is
\mathcal{C}
-semistable (resp.
\mathcal{C}
-stable) if

\rho
is
\zeta
-semistable (resp.
\zeta
-stable) for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}
.

Set
\mu^{-1}(0)^{\zeta}=\{B\in\mu^{-1}(0)\colon\zeta\text{-semistable}\}
.
Then the group
\prod_{v\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{GL}(V_{v})
acts
on
\mu^{-1}(0)^{\zeta}
naturally by conjugation.
Since we are considering the case where
V_{\infty}=\mathbb{C}
,
the gauge group
G=\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{GL}(V_{i})
can be identified
with
\prod_{v\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{GL}(V_{v})/\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{V}
by
normalizing the component in
\operatorname{GL}(V_{\infty})
to
\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{V_{\infty}}
,
noting that
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{V}
trivially acts on
\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}\nolimits_{Q}(V)
.
We set
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(V)=\mu^{-1}(0)^{\zeta}/G
in the sense of
GIT quotient.
Denoting the dimension vector by
\alpha=(\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{Q_{0}}
, we also write

M^{\zeta}(\alpha)=M_{Q}^{\zeta}(V)
by abuse of notation.
We also set
M^{\mathcal{C}}(\alpha)=M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}
.
Since elements in
G
are regarded

as
isomorphisms of framed quiver representations, which are
isomorphisms of quiver representations with the component on
framing vertex
\infty
equal to the identity,

M_{Q}^{\zeta}(V)
naturally regarded
as
moduli of framed quiver representations.
This trick is used in
[
10
]
to construct the Nakajima quiver variety
as a moduli space of quiver representations.

For each vertex
i\in I
, we consider a
tautological bundle

\mathcal{V}_{i}=\left(\{\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}\nolimits_{Q}(V)\mid\zeta\text{-semistable}\}\times V_{i}\right)/G

over
M_{Q}^{\zeta}(V)
.
We have a natural homomorphism of vector bundles
\phi_{h}\colon\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}
for each
arrow
h\in Q_{1}
such that

\phi_{h}|_{[B]}\colon\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}|_{[B]}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}|_{[B]}
is
identified with
b_{h}\colon V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)}\to V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}
.

2.2
Enhancement of quiver

A dimension vector
\beta=(\beta_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{I}

is said to be primitive if the greatest common divisor of
(\beta_{i})_{i\in I}
is equal to 1.
We fix a primitive dimension vector
\beta{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\Delta(\alpha)}
.
We choose a generic parameter
\bar{\zeta}\in\beta^{\perp}

such that
\bar{\zeta}
does not lie on any hyperplane
(\beta^{\prime})^{\perp}
other than
\beta^{\perp}
defined by

\beta^{\prime}\in\Delta(\alpha){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\{\beta\}}
.
By this
genericity assumption for
\bar{\zeta}
, we see that

\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
with
S_{\infty}=0
implies that

(\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits S_{i})_{i\in I}=d\beta\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{I}

for some integer
d\geq 0
.

Furthermore we have only two chambers whose boundaries contain
\bar{\zeta}
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{-}
denotes one of them such that
(\zeta^{-},\beta)<0
for any

\zeta^{-}\in\mathcal{C}^{-}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{+}
the other one.

Lemma 2.2
.

A
Q
-representation
(V,B)
is
\mathcal{C}^{-}
-stable (resp.
\mathcal{C}^{+}
-stable) if and only if
the following two conditions hold :

(a)
(V,B)
is
\bar{\zeta}
-semistable.

(b) We have no sub-representation
S
of
(V,B)
such that

\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
,
S_{\infty}=\mathbb{C}
and
S\neq V
(resp.
S_{\infty}=0
and
S=0
).

Proof.

We show that the negations of both sides are equivalent
by classifying non-trivial proper
Q_{0}
-graded subspace
S
of
V
such that
\zeta(S)>0
.
When
\pm\bar{\zeta}(S)>0
, we have
\pm\zeta(S)>0

since a stability parameter
\zeta
inside
\mathcal{C}^{-}
or
\mathcal{C}^{+}
can converge to
\bar{\zeta}
lying in the boundaries of
\mathcal{C}^{-}
and
\mathcal{C}^{+}
.
When
\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
and
S_{\infty}=\mathbb{C}
, we have
\zeta(S)=-\zeta(V/S)>0
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}

(resp.
\zeta(S)=-\zeta(V/S)<0
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
)
since the dimension vector of
V/S
is proportional to
\beta
.
Similarly when
\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
and
S_{\infty}=0
, we have
\zeta(S)<0
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}

(resp.
\zeta(S)>0
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
).
Hence existence of a sub-representation
S
of
(V,B)
with
\zeta(S)>0
,
meaning that
(V,B)
is not
\zeta
-stable, is equivalent to saying that
(a) or (b) does not hold.
∎

To describe wall-crossing from
\mathcal{C}^{-}
to
\mathcal{C}^{+}
, we choose a vertex
i\in I
such that
\beta_{i}\neq 0
.
Without loss of generality, such a fixed vertex is written by
\ast\in I
.
Then we define a new quiver

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{Q}_{1})

from
(Q_{0},Q_{1})
as follows.
The set of vertices in
\widetilde{Q}
is

\widetilde{Q}_{0}=Q_{0}\sqcup\{\ast(k)\mid k=1,2,\ldots,L\},

where
L\geq\alpha_{\ast}=\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\ast}
is a positive integer.
The set
\widetilde{Q}_{1}
of arrows in
\widetilde{Q}
is a disjoint union of
Q_{1}
and

\{\tilde{h}_{k}\colon\ast(k)\to\ast(k+1)\}_{k=1}^{L-1}\sqcup\{\tilde{h}_{L}\colon\ast(L)\to\ast\}.

We call such a quiver

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{Q}_{1})

an
enhancement
of

(Q_{0},Q_{1})
.

We also write by
\widetilde{Q}
a framed quiver
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{Q}_{1}},Q_{2},\infty)
.
We consider a
\widetilde{Q}_{0}
-graded vector space
\widetilde{V}=V\oplus\bigoplus_{k=1}^{L}\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k)}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(1)}\leq 1
,
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(L)}=\alpha_{\ast}=\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\ast}
, and

0\leq\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k)}-\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k-1)}\leq 1

for
k=2,\ldots,L-1
.
We set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}=\{k\in[L]\mid\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k)}-\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k-1)}=1\},

(22)

where

[L]=\{1,2,\ldots,L\}
.

Let us consider
\widetilde{Q}
-representations

\widetilde{B}=(\widetilde{b}_{h})_{h\in\widetilde{Q}_{1}}\colon A(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{Q}_{1})/\langle Q_{2}\rangle\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(\widetilde{V})
on
\widetilde{V}
satisfying the condition
that
linear maps

\widetilde{b}_{\tilde{h}_{k}}\colon\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k)}\to\widetilde{V}_{\ast(k+1)}
for
k=1,\ldots,L-1

and
\widetilde{b}_{\tilde{h}_{L}}\colon\widetilde{V}_{\ast(L)}\to V_{\ast}
corresponding to the all added arrows are injective.
Such
\widetilde{Q}
-representations are called

enhanced
Q
-representations
, which are regarded, up to
\prod_{k=1}^{L}\operatorname{GL}(V_{\ast(k)})
-action,
as pairs
(B,F_{\bullet})
of
Q
-representations
B

and full flags
F_{\bullet}
of
V_{\ast}
satisfying

\displaystyle\{i\in\mathbb{Z}\mid F_{i}/F_{i-1}\neq 0\}=\mathfrak{I}

(23)

via taking image of compositions
V_{\ast(k)}\to V_{\ast(k+1)}\to\cdots\to V_{\ast(L)}\to V_{\ast}
as
F_{k}
.
These elements
(B,F_{\bullet})
forms a full flag bundle of
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
over
M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
.

The stability parameters of
\widetilde{Q}
-representations
are taken from the augmented space as

\widetilde{\zeta}=(\zeta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{I}\times\mathbb{R}^{L}
,
where
\zeta=(\zeta_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
, and

\eta=(\eta_{\ast(k)})_{k=1}^{L}
belongs to
the component
\mathbb{R}^{L}
corresponding
to the added vertices

\ast(1),\ldots,\ast(L)\in\widetilde{Q}_{0}
.
We identify
\mathbb{R}^{I}
with the subset
\mathbb{R}^{I}\times\{0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{I}\times\mathbb{R}^{L}
.
We deform
\zeta^{-}=(\zeta^{-},0)\in\mathbb{R}^{I}\times\mathbb{R}^{L}
to get a parameter
\widetilde{\zeta}=(\zeta,\eta)
satisfying
conditions

[
38
, (9), (10)]
, and find
distinguished chambers
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\subset\mathbb{R}^{I}\times\mathbb{R}^{L}
containing

\widetilde{\zeta}
for
\ell=0,1,\ldots,L
,
which enhance the chamber
\mathcal{C}^{-},\mathcal{C}^{+}\subset\mathbb{R}^{I}
in the following sense.

Below, the
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stability conditions are interpreted in terms of enhanced
Q
representations
in Lemma
2.3
, and
we see that these conditions connect
\mathcal{C}^{-}
-stability (
\ell=0
) and

\mathcal{C}^{+}
-stability (
\ell=L
)
in Lemma
2.4
.

Lemma 2.3
.

For
\ell\geq 0
, an enhanced
Q
-representation
\widetilde{B}=(B,F_{\bullet})
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable if
B
is

\bar{\zeta}
-semistable and any sub-representation
S
of
B
with
\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
satisfies the following two conditions:

(a)

If
S_{\infty}=\mathbb{C}
and
S\neq V
, we have
F_{\ell}\not\subset S_{0}
.

(b)

If
S_{\infty}=0
and
S\neq 0
, we have
S_{0}\cap F_{\ell}=0
.

Proof.

See
[
38
, Definition 3.2, Proposition 3.3]
.
∎

For a dimension vector
\alpha\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{Q_{0}}
and a finite subset

\mathfrak{I}\subset[L]=\{1,2,\ldots,L\}
,
we write by
\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
moduli of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable enhanced

Q
-representations
(B,F_{\bullet})

on
V
satisfying (
19
) with full flags
F_{\bullet}
of

V_{\ast}
satisfying (
23
).
They are constructed as moduli
M^{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\widetilde{V})
of

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable
\widetilde{Q}
-representations
on
\widetilde{V}=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}\oplus\bigoplus_{k=1}^{L}V_{\ast(k)}
with
(
22
).

Lemma 2.4
.

When
\ell=0
(resp.
\ell=L
), a pair
\widetilde{B}=(B,F_{\bullet})
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable if and only
if
B
is
\zeta
-stable for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}
(resp.
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
).

Proof.

First we remark that
\zeta
-stability for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}
, or
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}

implies
\bar{\zeta}
-semi-stability since inside
\mathcal{C}^{-}
, or
\mathcal{C}^{+}
, we can take limit converging to
\bar{\zeta}
.
Under this assumption,
\zeta
-stability for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}
(resp.
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
)
is equivalent to prohibition of
sub-representation
S
with
\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
satisfying the assumption in (1) (resp. (2))
in Lemma
2.3
.
On the other hand, by the impossible conclusion
F_{0}=\{0\}\not\subset S_{0}
when
\ell=0

(resp.
S_{0}\cap F_{L}=S_{0}\cap V=0
when
\ell=L
), the prohibition is also equivalent
to the
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
-stability (resp.
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{L}
-stability) condition
under the assumption of
\bar{\zeta}
-semi-stability.
∎

We need notation.
In general, for a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
over
M
and a finite set
\mathfrak{I}\subset\mathbb{Z}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\nolimits\mathcal{E}
elements,
we write by
Fl_{M}(\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{I})
the full flag bundle over
M
whose fiber over
x\in M
consists of
full flags

F_{\bullet}=(F_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathcal{E}_{x}
satisfying (
23
).
These are all isomorphic to
Fl_{M}(\mathcal{E},[\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\nolimits\mathcal{E}])
, but we use products of these flag bundles
and combinatorial description in terms of the indices later.
We always set
F_{0}=0
.
When
\ell=0
(resp.
\ell=L
), we have
\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})\cong Fl_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I})
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{-}

(resp.
\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{+}
) by this lemma.
Taking cap product of the Euler class of the relative tangent bundle of

Fl_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I})\to M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
with the fundamental cycle
of
Fl_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I})
,
integrals over
M^{\zeta}(\alpha)

are reduced to ones over
Fl_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I})
.

2.3
Enhanced master space

Recall that we fix
0\leq\ell\leq L
and

\emptyset\neq\mathfrak{I}\subset[L]=\{1,\ldots,L\}
.
We want to recursively compute integrals over

\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
in terms of ones over

\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})

and
\widetilde{M}^{\ell^{\sharp}}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}\setminus\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
with

\ell^{\sharp}<\ell
and
non-empty subset
\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\subset\mathfrak{I}

where
d^{\sharp}=|\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}|/\beta_{\ast}

together with some coefficients defined by integral of cohomology classes (see (
38
) below).
To this end, we introduce the
enhanced master space

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(\ell,\alpha,\mathfrak{I},\theta^{-},\theta^{+})
containing these
moduli spaces (Definition
2.6
).

We fix a
Q_{0}
-graded vector space
V=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}
satisfying
(
19
),
and a
\widetilde{Q}_{0}
-graded vector space
\widetilde{V}=V\oplus\bigoplus_{k=1}^{L}V_{\ast(k)}
satisfying (
22
).
We set
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbb{M}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\widetilde{V})
.
The
map

\tilde{\mu}=\mu_{\widetilde{Q}}\colon\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\widetilde{V})=\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)

is obtained by composing the natural projection
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathbb{M}
and
\mu=\mu_{Q}\colon\mathbb{M}\to\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)
.
We also set
G=\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{GL}(V_{i})
and
\widetilde{G}=G\times\prod_{k=1}^{L}\operatorname{GL}(V_{k})
.
A moduli
\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable enhanced
\widetilde{Q}
-representations on
\widetilde{V}
is
constructed as a quotient stack
\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})=\tilde{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{\ell}/\widetilde{G}
,
where
\tilde{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{\ell}
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
-stable locus in
\tilde{\mu}^{-1}(0)
.

Let us take suitable stability parameters
\theta^{+}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{Q}_{0}}
and

\theta^{-}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{Q}_{0}}
.
From
\theta^{\pm}=(\theta^{\pm}_{i})_{i\in\widetilde{Q}_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{Q}_{0}}
, we define characters
\chi_{\pm}\colon\widetilde{G}\to\mathbb{C}^{\ast}

by sending
g=((g_{i})_{i\in I},(g_{k})_{k=1}^{L})\in\widetilde{G}
to

\chi_{\pm}(g)=\prod_{i\in I}(\det g_{i})^{\theta^{\pm}_{i}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{L}(\det g_{\ast(k)})^{\theta^{\pm}_{\ast(k)}}
.
We define weight spaces
\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\pm}}
by
g\cdot 1=\chi_{\pm}(g)
for
g\in\widetilde{G}
,
and
\widetilde{G}
-equivariant line bundles
L^{\pm}
over
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}
by
L^{\pm}=\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}\times\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\pm}}
.
Then we write by
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
the projective bundle
\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}}(L^{-}\oplus L^{+})
over
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}
, and
consider the hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)
(dual of the tautological bundle) over
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
.

We introduce the homogeneous coordinate
[x_{-},x_{+}]
of the fiber of
the projective bundle
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}}(L^{-}\oplus L^{+})
.
Recall that in Definition
2.1
is given a numerical criterion of GIT-stability for
Q
-representation.
On
\hat{\mathbb{M}}
, GIT-stability is defined as follows.

Definition 2.5
.

A point
\widehat{B}=(\widetilde{B},[x_{-},x_{+}])\in\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
with
\widetilde{B}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}
is said to be semi-stable if
there exists a section
\sigma\in\Gamma(\widehat{\mathbb{M}},\mathcal{O}(n))
such that
\sigma(\widehat{B})\neq 0
for a positive integer
n
,
where
\mathcal{O}(n)=\mathcal{O}(1)^{\otimes n}
.

We define a semi-stable locus
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{ss}=\{\widehat{B}\in\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\colon\text{ semi-stable }\}
.
We consider the composition
\hat{\mu}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)
of the projection
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\to\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}
and
\tilde{\mu}\colon\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)
.
Set
\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{ss}=\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)\cap\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{ss}
.

Definition 2.6
.

The quotient stack
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(\ell,\alpha,\mathfrak{I},\theta^{-},\theta^{+})=\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{ss}/\widetilde{G}
is called the
enhanced master space.

The enhanced master space
\mathcal{M}
has non-trivial
stabilizer groups as we remark
below Corollary
2.11
.
But we can sometimes take
stability parmeters
\theta^{\pm}
such
that
\mathcal{M}
is a variety with the
only trivial stabilizer
group as in
[
37
]
where
moduli space is empty set on one side of two chambers
along a wall.

We consider
the family
\theta^{t}=t\theta^{+}+(1-t)\theta^{-}
of stability parameters for
0\leq t\leq 1
.
We have the following description of points in
\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{ss}
.

Lemma 2.7
.

For
\widehat{B}=(\widetilde{B},[x_{-},x_{+}])\in\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)
, we have the following.

(a) When
x_{-}=0,
then
\widehat{B}
is semi-stable if and only if
\widetilde{B}
is
\theta^{+}
-semistable.

(b) When
x_{+}=0
, then
\widehat{B}
is semi-stable if and only if
\widetilde{B}
is
\theta^{-}
-semistable.

(c) When
x_{-}\cdot x_{+}\neq 0
, then
\widehat{B}
is semi-stable if and only if
\widetilde{B}
is
\theta^{t}
-semistable for some
0\leq t\leq 1
.

By (a) and (b) in Lemma
2.7
, we have the inclusion of

\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
and
\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
into
\mathcal{M}

as the hyperplane locus
\mathcal{M}_{\pm}=\{x_{\mp}=0\}
.
These spaces
\mathcal{M}_{+}\cong\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
and

\mathcal{M}_{-}\cong\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
are connected components of
fixed points set
\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}}
for
the fiber-wise
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
-action on
\mathcal{M}
induced by

\displaystyle e^{\hbar}\cdot[x_{-},x_{+}]=[e^{\hbar}x_{-},x_{+}]

(24)

for
e^{\hbar}\in\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
.

We consider
\mathcal{M}_{exc}=\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{+}\sqcup\mathcal{M}_{-})
.
A point of the quotient stack
\mathcal{M}
can be regarded as the orbit

\widetilde{G}\cdot\widehat{B}
, where the
\widetilde{G}
-action is defined by

g\cdot(\widetilde{B},[x_{-},x_{+}])=(g\cdot\widetilde{B},[\chi_{-}(g)x_{-},\chi_{+}(g)x_{+}])
for

g\in\widetilde{G}
.
This orbit is fixed by the fiberwise
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
-action if and only if
(\widetilde{B},[e^{\hbar}x_{-},x_{+}])
is contained in

\widetilde{G}\cdot(\widetilde{B},[x_{-},x_{+}])
.
When
x_{-}x_{+}\neq 0
, this implies existence of
g\in\tilde{G}

such that
g\cdot\widetilde{B}=\widetilde{B}
and
g\neq\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\tilde{V}}
,
meaning that
\widetilde{B}
has non-trivial stabilizer group in
\widetilde{G}
.

Depending on a choice of
\theta^{+}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
and
\theta^{-}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,
the line segment

\{\theta^{t}\mid 0\leq t\leq 1\}
hits different places in walls between
\widetilde{C}_{\ell}
and
\widetilde{C}_{0}
, and
determines a semi-stable locus
\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{ss}
by Lemma
2.7
.

Lemma 2.8
.

There exists
\theta^{+}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
and
\theta^{-}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
such that
for any
\widehat{B}=(\widetilde{B},[x_{0},x_{1}])\in\hat{\mu}^{-1}(0)^{ss}
, the following holds.
The
representations

\widetilde{B}
on
\widetilde{V}
has a non-trivial stabilizer group if and only if
we have decompositions

\widetilde{V}=\widetilde{V}^{\sharp}\oplus\widetilde{V}^{\flat}

and

\widetilde{B}=\widetilde{B}^{\sharp}\oplus\widetilde{B}^{\flat}

of representations
\widetilde{B}^{\sharp}

on
\widetilde{V}^{\sharp}
and
\widetilde{B}^{\flat}

on
\widetilde{V}^{\flat}

such that the following conditions hold.

(a) For
\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}=\{k\in\mathfrak{I}\mid\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V^{\sharp}_{\ast(k)}\neq\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V^{\sharp}_{\ast(k-1)}\}
,
we have
\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})\leq\ell
.

(b) We have
V^{\sharp}_{\infty}=0
and
\bar{\zeta}(V^{\sharp})=0
.

(c)
(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},\widetilde{B}^{\sharp})
is
(\bar{\zeta},0)
-semistable, and we have no sub-representation

S
of
(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},B^{\sharp})
such that
\bar{\zeta}(S)=0
,
S_{\ast(\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}))}\neq 0

and
S\neq\widetilde{V}^{\sharp}
.

(d)
V^{\flat}_{\infty}=V_{\infty}
and
(\widetilde{V}^{\flat},\widetilde{B}^{\flat})
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}
-stable.

Objects
(\widetilde{V}^{\flat},\widetilde{B}^{\flat})
appearing in (d) in Lemma
2.8
are parametrized by

\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}\setminus\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
.
In the next subsection, we construct a moduli space parametrizing
(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},\widetilde{B}^{\sharp})

satisfying (a), (b), (c) in Lemma
2.8
.
The connected components of this moduli space are indexed by an element in the following set

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})=\{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\subset\mathfrak{I}\mid|\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}|=d^{\sharp}\beta_{\ast}\text{ for }d^{\sharp}=1,\ldots,\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor\text{ and }\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})\leq\ell\}.

(25)

2.4
Moduli stack of destabilizing

representations
\widetilde{B}^{\sharp}

on
\widetilde{V}^{\sharp}

From the condition (b) in Lemma
2.8
, we need a new framing vertex other than
\infty\in Q_{0}
to regard

(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},\widetilde{B}^{\sharp})
as a framed quiver representation.
Comparing the condition (c) in Lemma
2.8
with the condition (b) in Lemma
2.2
, we take
\ast(\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}))\in\widetilde{Q}_{0}
as
a framing vertex instead of
\infty\in Q_{0}
.
This leads to a new framed quiver
Q^{\sharp}=(Q^{\sharp},R,\infty^{\sharp})
as follows.
We define
Q^{\sharp}=(Q^{\sharp}_{0},Q^{\sharp}_{1})
by
Q^{\sharp}_{0}=Q_{0}\sqcup\{\infty^{\sharp}\},Q^{\sharp}_{1}=Q_{1}\sqcup\{a^{\sharp}\colon\infty^{\sharp}\to\ast\}

adding a new arrow
\infty^{\sharp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{\sharp}}}{{\to}}\ast
.
We only consider
Q^{\sharp}_{0}
-graded vector space

V^{\sharp}\oplus V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
where
V^{\sharp}=\bigoplus_{i\in I}V^{\sharp}_{i}

such that
(\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V^{\sharp}_{i})_{i\in I}=d^{\sharp}\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
and

V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}=\mathbb{C}.

For any parameter
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
, we define
stability parameter
\zeta^{\sharp}=(\zeta^{\sharp}_{v})_{v\in Q^{\sharp}_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{Q^{\sharp}_{0}}

by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\zeta^{\sharp}_{i}=\zeta_{i}\text{ for }i\in I\\
\zeta^{\sharp}_{\infty}=0\\
\zeta^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}=-\sum_{i\in I}\zeta_{i}\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V^{\sharp}_{i}=-\zeta(d^{\sharp}\beta).\end{cases}

(26)

We set
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)=M^{\zeta^{\sharp}}_{Q^{\sharp}}(V^{\sharp}\oplus V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}})

for
\zeta\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}^{-}}
.

Remark 2.9
.

From our choice (
26
) and
\zeta\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}^{-}}
, the moduli space

H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
parametrizes

Q^{\sharp}
-representations satisfying conditions (a) and (b) in Lemma
2.2

replacing
\infty
with
\infty^{\sharp}
.
When
Q
is
the double of
the Jordan quiver together with the
preprojective relation

giving constructions of the Nakajima quiver variety of
A^{(1)}_{0}
-type,

H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
is isomorphic to the Hilbert scheme of points on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
.

We consider a tautological homomorphism
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}

on
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
corresponding to the arrow from
\infty^{\sharp}
to
\ast
.
It is a non-zero homomorphism from the line bundle
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
by the above remark.
In fact, otherwise we can take a sub-representation
V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\subset V^{\sharp}\oplus V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}

violating the condition (b) in Lemma
2.2
.
We set
\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast}=\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}/\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
,
and consider the full flag bundle
Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
over
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
where

\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp}=\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\setminus\{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})\}
.
An element in
Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})

is represented by a collection
(B^{\sharp},a^{\sharp},\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet})

of
\zeta^{\sharp}
-stable
Q
-representation
B^{\sharp}
on
V^{\sharp}
,
a injection
a^{\sharp}\colon V^{\sharp}_{\infty}=\mathbb{C}\to V^{\sharp}_{\ast}
,
and a full flag
\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}
of
\bar{V}^{\sharp}=V^{\sharp}_{\ast}/a^{\sharp}(V^{\sharp}_{\infty})
.
It is identified with an object
(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},\widetilde{B}^{\sharp})
which appeared in
Lemma
2.8
as follows.

We recall that
\widetilde{Q}
-representations
(\widetilde{V}^{\sharp},\widetilde{B}^{\sharp})
are regarded as
enhanced
Q
-representations
(B^{\sharp},F^{\sharp}_{\bullet})
as in §
2.2
.
On the other hand, from the above collection
(B^{\sharp},a^{\sharp},\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet})
,
we set
F^{\sharp}_{\bullet}
as follows.
For
i\geq\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
, we write
by
F^{\sharp}_{i}
the inverse images of
\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{i}
by
the quotient map
V^{\sharp}_{\ast}\to\bar{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}
.
For
i<\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
, we set
F^{\sharp}_{i}=0
.
This also gives enhanced
Q
-representations
(B^{\sharp},F^{\sharp})
, and
we have
F^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\in Fl(V^{\sharp}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
since

\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\in Fl(\bar{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
.

Lemma 2.10
.

For a collection
(B^{\sharp},a^{\sharp},\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet})
of a
Q
-representation
B^{\sharp}
on
V^{\sharp}
,
an injection
a^{\sharp}\colon V^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\to V^{\sharp}_{\ast}
,
and a full flag
\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\in Fl(\bar{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
,
the corresponding data

(B^{\sharp},F^{\sharp}_{\bullet})
satisfies condition (c) in Lemma
2.8

if and only if
(B^{\sharp},a^{\sharp})

represents an element in
Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})

Proof.

Since
F^{\sharp}_{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})}=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits a^{\sharp}
, it follows from comparison between
the condition (c) in Lemma
2.8
and
the condition (b) in Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Corollary 2.11
.

There exists a set theoretical bijection between
\mathcal{M}_{exc}
and

\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}),

where we set
d^{\sharp}=\lvert\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\rvert/\beta_{\ast}
and
\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}=\mathfrak{I}\setminus\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}

for
\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}
in the index set
\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})
defined in (
25
).

Proof.

For each
\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})
, we take
data
((B^{\sharp},a^{\sharp},\bar{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}),(B^{\flat},F^{\flat}))
representing elements in

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}).

From these data we get representatives

((B^{\sharp},F^{\sharp}_{\bullet}),(B^{\flat},F^{\flat}),[1,1])
of elements in
\mathcal{M}_{exc}
.
By Lemma
2.8
and Lemma
2.10
, this gives a well-defined bijection.
∎

This corollary suggest that
\mathcal{M}_{exc}
have connected components
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
indexed by

\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})
such that points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
are
parametrized by
Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})
.
But we have a non-trivial stabilizer group of
((B^{\sharp},F^{\sharp}_{\bullet}),(B^{\flat},F^{\flat}),[1,1])

consisting of
\zeta\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{V^{\sharp}}
for the primitive
m^{\sharp}
-th root

\zeta=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}/m^{\sharp}}
of unity for

m^{\sharp}=d^{\sharp}\theta^{+}(\beta)-d^{\sharp}\theta^{-}(\beta)
.
Hence we need to take étale cover
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}

of degree
\frac{1}{m^{\sharp}}
.
Precise statements are given in Theorem
2.12

in the next subsection.

We consider a modified action

\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}}}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
induced by

\displaystyle\left(\rho,F_{\bullet},[x_{-},x_{+}]\right)\mapsto\left(e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\tilde{V}^{\sharp}\oplus}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\tilde{V}^{\flat}}\right)\left(\rho,F_{\bullet},[e^{\hbar}x_{-},x_{+}]\right).

(27)

This action is equal to the original
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
-action (
24
), since the difference is absorbed in
G
-action.

2.5
Decomposition of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}}

For tautological bundles
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp},\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
over
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)

and
\mathcal{V}^{\flat}
over
\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})
, we also
write by the same letter their pull-backs to

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})

by the projections.
We take universal full flags
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
on

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
.
For
i\geq\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
, we write
by
\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{i}
the inverse images of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\sharp}_{i}
by
the tautological homomorphism
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}
, and
set
\mathcal{F}_{i}=0
for
i<\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})
.

Theorem 2.12
.

We have a decomposition into connected components

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}}=\mathcal{M}_{+}\sqcup\mathcal{M}_{-}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{\mathcal{}}D_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}

(28)

such that the followings hold.

(i) We have
\mathcal{M}_{+}\cong\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})
and
\mathcal{M}_{-}
is isomorphic to the full flag bundle

Fl_{M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast},\mathfrak{I})
of the tautological bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
on
M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)

with
\{k\in[L]\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}/\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\}=\mathfrak{I}
.

(ii) For each
\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{\mathcal{}}D_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})
, we have
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar/m}
-equivariant
finite étale morphisms

\Phi\colon\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
of degree
1/m^{\sharp}
, and

\Psi\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\prime}\to Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})

of degree
1/(m^{\sharp})^{2}
, where

\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}=\mathfrak{I}\setminus\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}
,
m^{\sharp}=d^{\sharp}\theta^{+}(\beta)-d^{\sharp}\theta^{-}(\beta)
, and

d^{\sharp}=\lvert\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\rvert/\beta_{\ast}
.

(iii) As
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar/m^{\sharp}}
-equivariant

Q_{0}
-graded vector bundles on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\prime}
,
we have a decomposition

\displaystyle\Phi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}}

\displaystyle\cong\left(\Psi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}\otimes L\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}}}\right)\oplus\Psi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\flat}

\displaystyle\Phi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}}

\displaystyle\cong\left(\Psi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes L\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}}}\right)\oplus\Psi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet},

where
L
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

See
[
38
, Theorem 4.3]
.
∎

The component
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}

is constructed as a quotient stack

U_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}/G_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
, where

U_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}/G_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
is
a Zariski open subset of an Affine space, and
G_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
is
a product of general linear groups
acting on
U_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}

with the finite stabilizer groups.
Then the étale cover
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\prime}
in Theorem
2.12
is obtained by
replacing a torus component

\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}
in
G_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
with

\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t^{1/m^{\sharp}}}

(see proof of
[
38
, Theorem 4.3 (iii)]
).
This gives non-integer degree

1/m^{\sharp}
by

[
46
, Definition 1.15]
.

We write by
N_{+},N_{-}
, and
N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
normal bundles of
\mathcal{M}_{+},\mathcal{M}_{-}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
in
\mathcal{M}
respectively.

Lemma 2.13

(
[
26
, Proposition 5.9.2]
)
.

We have

N_{\pm}=e^{\pm\hbar}\otimes\bigotimes_{i\in\widetilde{Q}_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}}\det\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\otimes\mp(\theta^{+}_{i}-\theta^{-}_{i})}
.

We take universal full flags
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
on

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
,
and
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat}
on
M_{\widetilde{Q}}^{\bar{\zeta},\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}_{\sharp})-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\flat})
.
On
Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})
,
we define a full flag
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
of
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}

by the pull-back of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
to
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}
for
i\neq\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})

and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})}^{\sharp}=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast})
.
We also write their pull-backs to the product
\mathcal{M}_{\sharp}\times\mathcal{M}_{\flat}

by the same letter
\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet}
.

For vector bundles
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
on a stack
\mathcal{Z}
, we write by

\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{Z}
.
For two flags
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime}
of sheaves on the same Deligne-Mumford stack,
we set

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l<l^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}om\left(\mathcal{F}_{l}/\mathcal{F}_{l-1},\mathcal{F}_{l^{\prime}}^{\prime}/\mathcal{F}_{l^{\prime}-1}^{\prime}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime})+\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}).

When
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\prime}
, we set
\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet})=\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet})
.

Lemma 2.14
.

We have
\Phi^{\ast}N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}\cong\Psi^{\ast}\left[\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}\otimes L\otimes e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}},\mathcal{V}^{\flat})+\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes L\otimes e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}},\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet})\right]
where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp},\mathcal{V}^{\flat})

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})-\sum_{i\in I}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{i})\right),

(29)

where the sum is taken for only cross terms

(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)=(\sharp,\flat),(\flat,\sharp)
.

Here
m^{\sharp}
is the parameter appeared in
Theorem
2.12
(ii).

2.6
Relative tangent bundles for flags

We consider the pull-back
\Theta^{rel}
to
\mathcal{M}
of
the relative tangent bundle of
[\widetilde{\mu}^{-1}(0)/G]

over
[\mu^{-1}(0)/G]
.
After restricting to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
and
pulling back to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\prime}
, we have a
decomposition

\displaystyle\Phi^{\ast}\Theta^{rel}=\Psi^{\ast}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})\oplus\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat})\oplus\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}\otimes L\otimes e^{\hbar/m^{\sharp}},\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat}))

(30)

We also consider the relative tangent bundle
\Theta^{\prime}
of
the projection

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\to H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
.
Then we have an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Theta^{\prime}\to\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})\to\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast}\to 0,

(31)

where
\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}_{\ast}
is the quotient
by the tautological homomorphism
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}
,
and it is identified with
\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})},\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\ast}/\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})})
.

3
Wall-crossing formula

We set
M^{0}_{Q}(\alpha)=M^{0}(\alpha)=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits\Gamma(\mathbb{M}(V),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{M}(V)})^{G}
, and write by

\Pi=\Pi_{Q}\colon M^{\zeta}(\alpha)\to M^{0}(\alpha)

the natural projection.

3.1
Equivariant framed quiver

We introduce an algebraic torus
\mathbb{T}=(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{n}

and use torus
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant Chow rings to define
integrals in general.
For a character
\chi\colon\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
,
we write by
\mathbb{C}_{\chi}
the weight space,
that is, the one dimensional
\mathbb{T}
-representation
with the eigenvalue
\chi
.
For the coordinates
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
of

\mathbb{T}
, we regard a monomial
s_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{m_{n}}
a character
\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
sending

p\in\mathbb{T}
to
s_{1}(p)^{m_{1}}\cdots s_{n}(p)^{m_{n}}
.
The monomial
s_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{m_{n}}
is also
used as an abbreviation of the weight space

\mathbb{C}_{s_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{m_{n}}}
.
