In particular, the
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant Chow ring

A_{\mathbb{T}}=A_{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}(\text{pt})
over the point pt,
is isomorphic to the polynomial ring

\mathbb{Z}[\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}]
, where

\xi_{1}=c_{1}(s_{1}),\ldots,\xi_{n}=c_{1}(s_{n})

are called the
equivariant parameters
.

We take a pair
(Q,\chi_{Q})
where

\chi_{Q}=((\chi_{h})_{h\in Q_{1}},(\chi_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in Q_{2}})

is a collection of
characters of
\mathbb{T}
.
We replace (
20
) with

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)=\prod_{h\in Q_{1}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{h}},\quad\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)=\prod_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},V_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\gamma}}.

(32)

Definition 3.1
.

A pair
(Q,\chi_{Q})
is called a

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant framed quiver
when the
map

\mu=\mu_{Q}\colon\mathbb{M}_{Q}(V)\to\mathbb{L}_{Q}(V)
is
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant for the

\mathbb{T}
-action induced from (
32
).

For a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant framed quiver

Q=(Q,\chi_{Q})
, we have natural
\mathbb{T}
-action on
moduli spaces
M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
of
\zeta
-semistable

Q
-representations for any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
.
Furthermore
\Pi_{Q}\colon M^{\zeta}(\alpha)\to M^{0}(\alpha)
are
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant.
In the following, we assume that
the fixed points sets
M^{0}_{Q}(\alpha)^{\mathbb{T}}
are
isomorphic to the point
pt
for
any framed quiver
Q
.
In particular,
Q^{\sharp}=(Q^{\sharp},\chi_{Q^{\sharp}})
are regarded as

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant framed quiver with

\chi_{Q^{\sharp}}
naturally induced from

\chi_{Q}
.

3.2
Equivariant Rieman-Roch

We recall Riemann-Roch theorem for equivariant setting
from
[
12
]
.
We consider a Deligne-Mumford stack
X
with a

\mathbb{T}
-action.
Let
CH^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
denote the

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
Chow group of
X
,

A^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
the
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant Chow ring
of
X
,
and
K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
the Grothendieck group of

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant coherent sheaves on
X
.
When
X
is smooth, we identify

CH^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
with
A^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
,
and
K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
with the Grothendieck ring

K_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
of

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
vector bundles on
X
.

When
X
is a point
pt
, then
K^{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})
is
equal to the

\mathbb{T}
-representation ring

R_{\mathbb{T}}=\mathbb{Z}[s_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,s_{n}^{\pm 1}]
.
Here
(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
is the coordinate of

\mathbb{T}=(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{n}
, and
for
l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
the monomial

s_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{l_{n}}

denotes the weight space
\mathbb{C}_{s_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{l_{n}}}

with the eigen-value

s_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{l_{n}}
.
We can also see that
CH^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})=A^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})
is equal to
the polynomial ring
\mathbb{Z}[\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}]
where

\xi_{1}=c_{1}(s_{1}),\ldots,\xi_{n}=c_{1}(s_{n})
.

For a subset

S_{\mathbb{T}}=\{1-s_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots s_{n}^{l_{n}}\mid(l_{1},\ldots,l_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n+1}\setminus\{0\}\},

we consider the ideal
I_{\mathbb{T}}=\langle S_{\mathbb{T}}\rangle
of
R_{\mathbb{T}}
generated by
S_{\mathbb{T}}
, and
write by
\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{T}}
the completion of
R_{\mathbb{T}}

along
I_{\mathbb{T}}
.
We set
\widehat{K}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)=K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\otimes_{R_{\mathbb{T}}}\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{T}}
, and

\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)=\prod_{i\geq 0}CH^{i}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
.
In particular, we set

A_{\mathbb{T}}=\mathbb{Z}[e^{\pm\xi_{1}},\ldots,e^{\pm\xi_{n}}]\subset\widehat{CH}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}(\text{pt})=\mathbb{Z}[[\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}]].

We set
Td=Td_{X}=\frac{\text{Eu}(TX)}{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(TX)}
,
and define a map
\tau=\tau_{X}\colon K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\to\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
by

\tau(\mathcal{E})=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits(\mathcal{E})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Td}}\nolimits_{X}\right)\cap[X]

for
\mathcal{E}\in K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
.
The map
\tau
is compatible
with push-forwards, and
factors through the induced isomorphism

\displaystyle\widehat{K}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\cong\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X).

(33)

We often use identification

R_{\mathbb{T}}\cong A_{\mathbb{T}}

via the exponential map
\tau_{\text{pt}}
.

By the localization theorem
[
45
, Theorem 2.1]
,
we have an isomorphism

S_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}K^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\cong S_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}K^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}})
.
Using (
33
), we also have

T_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)\cong T_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}})
,
where

T_{\mathbb{T}}=\tau(S_{\mathbb{T}})=\{1-e^{l_{1}\xi_{1}+\cdots+l_{n}\xi_{n}}\mid(l_{1},\ldots,l_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\setminus\{0\}\}.

In our setting, we have the diagram:

\textstyle{K^{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}(\alpha))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}(\alpha))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{\ast}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathbb{T}}(M^{0}(\alpha))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{M^{0}(\alpha)}}
\scriptstyle{(\iota_{\ast})^{-1}}
\textstyle{\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{0}(\alpha))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\iota_{\ast})^{-1}}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}K^{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\text{pt}}}
\textstyle{T_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\widehat{CH}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})}

where
\iota\colon\text{pt}=M^{0}(\alpha)^{\mathbb{T}}\to M^{0}(\alpha)
is the embedding.

3.3
Virtual fundamental cycle

We recall constructions of virtual fundamental cycles
[
8
]
in the following setting.
We introduce a triple
\mathcal{K}=(Y,P,\sigma)

called
Kuranishi structure
originated from

[
13
]
.

Here
Y=(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1))
is a smooth scheme with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1)
,
P
is a group scheme acting on
(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1))
, and
\sigma\colon Y\to E
is a
P
-invariant section of
P
-equivariant vector bundle
E
over
Y
.
We take a semistable locus
Y^{ss}
of
Y
with respect to
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1)
, and put
Z=\sigma^{-1}(0)\cap Y^{ss}
.
We assume that any point in
Y^{ss}
has a finite stabilizer group.
Hence
\mathcal{Y}=[Y^{ss}/P]
is a Deligne-Mumford stack.

We define a moduli stack
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{K}}=[Z/P]
as the quotient
stack,

and say that
\mathcal{K}=(Y,P,\sigma)

gives a
Kuranishi structure
of
\mathcal{Z}
.
In our case,
E
is always a trivial bundle
Y\times\mathbb{C}^{l}
, and we have
\sigma=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{Y}\times\varphi\in\Gamma(Y,E)

where
\varphi\colon Y\to\mathbb{C}^{l}
is a
P
-invariant function.
In such a case, we just write
\mathcal{K}=(Y,P,\varphi)
instead of
\sigma=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{Y}\times\varphi
.

To define
virtual fundamental cycle

[\mathcal{Z}]^{vir}
, we consider
a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}=[(E|_{Y^{ss}})/P]
over
\mathcal{Y}
.
We consider the zero section
0\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{E}

and write by the same letter
\sigma\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{E}

the section induced by
\sigma\in\Gamma(Y,E)
.
We also consider a fundamental cycle
[\mathcal{Y}]
of smooth Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{Y}
.
For example, we can take a Kuranishi structure
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{M}}=(\widehat{\mathbb{M}},\widetilde{G},\hat{\mu})

of the enhanced master space
\mathcal{M}

as we saw in Definition
2.6
.

Using these we define the virtual fundamental cycle of
\mathcal{Z}
by
[\mathcal{Z}]^{vir}=0^{!}[\mathcal{Y}]
,
where
0^{!}
is the refined Gysin homomorphism by the zero section
0\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{E}

in the following diagram :

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.43303pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.09721pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.09721pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{E}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.05556pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.51387pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{0}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.5pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

We take another Kuranishi structure
\mathcal{K}_{1}=(Y_{1},P_{1},\sigma_{1})
of another stack

\mathcal{Z}_{1}=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
where
\sigma_{1}\in\Gamma(Y_{1},E_{1})
and
E_{1}

is a vector bundle on
Y_{1}
.
We assume that there exists a bundle morphism

E_{1}\to E
equivariant via a group homomorphism

P_{1}\to P
such that sections
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma
coincide
via the isomorphism
E_{1}\to E|_{Y_{1}}
.
This collection of morphisms are called a morphism of Kuranishi structures.
Furthermore when these morphisms induces an étale morphism
[Y_{1}^{ss}/P_{1}]\to[Y^{ss}/P]
,
we call such a collection of morphisms a
coordinate change
of Kuranishi structures.

When we have a morphism
\mathcal{K}_{1}=(Y_{1},P_{1},\sigma_{1})\to\mathcal{K}=(Y,P,\sigma)
of Kuranishi structures, such that
Y_{1}\to Y
is flat, then we have

[\mathcal{Z}_{1}]=\Psi^{\ast}[\mathcal{Z}]
.
In general, virtual fundamental cycles can be different depending on choice of Kuranishi chatrts.

We remark that we can always take Kuranishi structure consisting of
single
Kuranishi neighborhood
globally covering a moduli stack
\mathcal{Z}
in our setting.
In general, we need an open covering
of
\mathcal{Z}
consisting of
Kuranishi neighborhoods as in

[
13
]
,
[
14
]
.
In algebro-geometric setting, we have obstruction theory developed in
[
8
]
to construct virtual fundamental cycle in general case.

3.4
K
-theoretic integral

Given a collection of
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant framed quivers

(Q,\chi_{Q}),(Q^{(1)},\chi_{Q^{(1)}}),\ldots,(Q^{(j)},\chi_{Q^{(j)}})
,
we take generic stability parameter

\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{Q\setminus\{\infty\}}
, and

\zeta^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}^{Q^{(k)}\setminus\{\infty\}}
for
k=1,\ldots,j
.
We consider moduli spaces

M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)

for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q^{\infty}_{0}}
, and

M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})

for
\alpha^{(k)}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q^{(k)}_{0}}

for
k=1,\ldots,j
.
We consider the products

\Pi=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}\Pi_{Q^{(k)}}\colon M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\to M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{0}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})

The products
\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})

and
M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})

of moduli
spaces carry

virtual fundamental cycles

\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}

and

\left[M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}
defined as in

[
38
, 5.1]
.

For a cohomology class
C\in A^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}\left(M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right)
, we define an
integral of
C
along the virtual fundamental cycle

\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}

as an element

\int_{\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}}C=\left(\text{id}\otimes\bigotimes_{k=1}^{j}\iota^{(k)}_{\ast}\right)^{-1}\left(\Pi_{\ast}\left(C\cap\left[M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)\times\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}\right)\right)

in

T_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}CH^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha))
.
Here
\iota^{(k)}\colon M^{0}(\alpha^{(k)})^{\mathbb{T}}\to M^{0}(\alpha^{(k)})
is the embedding for
k=1,\ldots,j
.

Definition 3.2
.

For a cohomology class
C\in A^{\bullet}(M_{\boldsymbol{d}})
,
we define an
K
-theoretic integral of
C
along the
virtual fundamental cycle
\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}

as an element

\int^{[K]}_{\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}}C=\int_{\left[\prod_{k=1}^{j}M^{\zeta^{(k)}}_{Q^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})\right]^{vir}}C\cdot\boxtimes_{k=1}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Td}}\nolimits_{M_{Q^{(k)}}^{\zeta^{(k)}}(\alpha^{(k)})}

in

T_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}CH^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha))
.

For later use, we set
\mathbb{T}_{0}=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}
, and consider

\mathbb{T}_{0}
-action on
M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
via
the projection
\mathbb{T}_{0}=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}\to\mathbb{T}
, that is, the component
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}
trivially
acts.
For a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
with the
rank
r
, we set

\wedge_{-t}\mathcal{E}=\sum_{i=0}^{r}(-1)^{i}\wedge^{i}\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{t}\right)=\sum_{i=0}^{r}(-t)^{i}\wedge^{i}\mathcal{E}

For
K
-theory class
\Lambda=[\mathcal{E}]-[\mathcal{F}]

with vector bundles
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
, we set

\wedge_{-t}\Lambda=\wedge_{-t}E/\wedge_{-t}F\in S_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}^{-1}R_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}
.
We further define the
K
-theoretic Euler class of

\Lambda=[\mathcal{E}]-[\mathcal{F}]\in K_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}(\alpha))
by

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits(\wedge_{-t}\Lambda^{\vee})={\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\left(\wedge_{-t}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\right)\over\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\left(\wedge_{-t}\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\right)}.\in T_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}^{-1}\widehat{A}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}(M^{\zeta}(\alpha)).

When we can substitute
t=1
, we set

\wedge_{-1}\Lambda=\wedge_{-t}\Lambda\Big|_{t=1}
and

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\Lambda\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\Lambda\right)\Big|_{t=1}
.

For polynomials
F\left(\left(X_{i,1},\ldots,X_{i,m}\right)_{i\in I},\left(Y_{i,1},\ldots,Y_{i,m}\right)_{i\in I}\right)

with the coefficients in

S_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}^{-1}R_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}

and

I
-graded vector bundle
\mathcal{E}=\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, set

F\left(\mathcal{E}\right):=F\left(\left(\wedge^{1}\mathcal{E}_{i},\ldots,\wedge^{m}\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)_{i\in I},\left(\wedge^{1}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\vee},\ldots,\wedge^{m}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\vee}\right)_{i\in I}\right).

In more general, we consider
K
-theory classes

F(\mathcal{V})
for tautological bundles

\mathcal{V}
on
M^{\zeta}(\alpha)
and cohomology classes

C=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})
.
Then we have

\int^{[K]}_{M^{\zeta}(\alpha)}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}F(\mathcal{V})}\in T_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}^{-1}A_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}\cong S_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}^{-1}R_{\mathbb{T}_{0}}
.
When
F(\mathcal{V})=\wedge_{-t}\Lambda^{\vee}
for

\Lambda=\Lambda(\mathcal{V})
, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda)
.

For simplicity, we assume that

M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha),M^{\zeta^{\sharp}}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\alpha^{\sharp})
are
smooth,
the fixed points sets

M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)^{\mathbb{T}},M^{\zeta^{\sharp}}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\alpha^{\sharp})
are finite, and the
virtual fundamental cycles

[M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)]^{vir}
coincide with
usual ones
[M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)]
.
We note that
[M^{\zeta^{\sharp}}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\alpha^{\sharp})]^{vir}

can be different from

[M^{\zeta^{\sharp}}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\alpha^{\sharp})]
.
We often identify
cohomology classes
C
on
M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)
and
the Poincare dual
C\cap[M^{\zeta}_{Q}(\alpha)]
.

3.5
Localization for an enhanced master space
\mathcal{M}

For a wall-crossing formula, we consider

\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
-action on

\mathcal{M}
defined in (
24
), and set

\mathbb{T}_{1}=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar}
.
Using
[
16
, (1)]
, we consider the following
commutative diagram :

\textstyle{K^{\mathbb{T}_{1}}(\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{\ast}}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbb{T}_{1}}^{-1}K^{\mathbb{T}_{1}}(\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{S_{\mathbb{T}_{1}}^{-1}K^{\mathbb{T}_{1}}(M_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The middle upper horizontal arrow in the above diagram is given by

{\iota_{+}^{\ast}\over\wedge_{-1}(N_{+})^{\vee}}+{\iota_{-}^{\ast}\over\wedge_{-1}(N_{-})^{\vee}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}{\iota_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}^{\ast}\over\wedge_{-1}(N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}})^{\vee}}.

Here
\iota_{\pm}
and
\iota_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}
are

\mathcal{M}_{\pm}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}

\mathcal{M}
.

We consider a
K
-theory class
F(\mathcal{V})\in K_{\mathbb{T}_{1}}(\mathcal{M})
as above, and
consider
C=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})
.
By the above diagram, we have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})=\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{+}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{+})}+\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{-}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{-}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{-})}+\sum_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}})}.

(34)

Since
\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})
is holomorphic in
\hbar
, we have

\displaystyle 0=\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{+}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{-}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{+})}+\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{-}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{-}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{-})}+\sum_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}})}

(35)

for contour integrals over arbitrary closed path.

We set
\widetilde{F}(\mathcal{V})=F(\mathcal{V})\cdot\wedge_{-t}\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet})^{\vee}
.
When we consider (
126
), we set
t=1
.
Then substituting
\widetilde{C}=\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\widetilde{F}(\mathcal{V})/|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!

we compute each term in (
35
).
Here we introduce
[m]_{t}=(1-t^{m})/(1-t)
for a non-negative integer
m
,
and we set
|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}=[|\mathfrak{I}|]_{t}
for a finite set
\mathfrak{I}
.
We also set
[m]_{t}!=[1]_{t}\cdot[2]_{t}\cdots[m]_{t}
, and

c=\sum_{i\in\widetilde{Q}_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}}(\theta^{+}_{i}-\theta^{-}_{i})c_{1}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
.

To compute further, we delete line bundles
L
and integers
m^{\sharp}
in Theorem
2.12

(iii) and Lemma
2.14
by taking residues

\displaystyle\oint f(\hbar)d\hbar=m^{\sharp}\oint f(m^{\sharp}\hbar+a)d\hbar

(cf.
[
34
, §8.2]
).
Then we have

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{-}]^{vir}}{\widetilde{C}|_{\mathcal{M}_{-}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{-})}

\displaystyle=\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\otimes e^{\hbar}})\over 1-e^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hbar}}\cdot{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}))\over|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!}

\displaystyle=\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\otimes e^{\hbar}})\over 1-e^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hbar}}

\displaystyle=-\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})=-\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}C.

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{+}]^{vir}}{\widetilde{C}|_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{+})}

\displaystyle=\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\otimes e^{\hbar}})\over 1-e^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\hbar}}\cdot{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}))\over|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!}

\displaystyle={1\over|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}))=\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\int_{[\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha,\mathfrak{I})]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}-\int_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}C=-\sum_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\ast}}^{\ell}(\mathfrak{I})}\oint d\hbar\int^{[K]}_{[\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\widetilde{F}(\mathcal{V}))|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}}}\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(N_{\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}})}.

(36)

This coefficient
m^{\sharp}
vanishes by comparing integrals over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{I}_{\sharp}}
and

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})\times\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})
via
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{I}}
in Theorem
2.12
.
Then together with (
30
),
the last summand in (
36
) is equal to

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar{|\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}|_{t}!\over|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d^{\sharp}\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\flat})]^{vir}}{1\over|\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}|_{t}!}

\displaystyle\cdot\int^{[K]}_{[Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\left[F(\mathcal{V}^{\flat}+\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}})\cdot\wedge_{-t}\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}}+\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet})^{\vee}\right]\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left[\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}},\mathcal{V}^{\flat})+\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}},\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet})\right]}.

(37)

Here we have

\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}}+\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet})=\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})+\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat})+\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}_{\bullet}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar}},\mathcal{F}^{\flat}_{\bullet})
,
and further decomposition
\Theta(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})=\Theta(\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})+\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp}
with the relative tangent bundle

\Theta(\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}^{\sharp})
of

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{\sharp},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}^{\sharp})

over
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp}\beta)
from (
31
).

3.6
Iterated classes

In the following, we deduce wall-crossing formula from analysis
in the previous section.
These are the similar calculations to
[
33
, §6]
,
hence we omit detail explanation.

In the following, we fix a
Q_{0}
-graded vector space

V=\bigoplus_{v\in Q_{0}}V_{v}

with
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{\infty}=1
.
We set
\alpha=(\alpha_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}=(\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{Q_{0}}
.
For
\boldsymbol{d}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}
,
we set
|\boldsymbol{d}|=d^{(1)}+\cdots+d^{(j)}
.
Let
\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}

be the set of collections

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})

(decomposition data) such that

\bullet

\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)}
are
disjoint non-empty subsets of
[\alpha_{\ast}]=\{1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{\ast}\}
,

\bullet

|\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}|=d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}
with
d^{(i)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}

for
i=1,\ldots,j
, and

\bullet

\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)})>\cdots>\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})
.

We set
\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=\frac{1}{\beta_{\ast}}(|\mathfrak{I}^{(1)}|,\ldots,|\mathfrak{I}^{(j)}|)=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}

and
\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}=[\alpha_{\ast}]\setminus\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{j}\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}
.
We note that
\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},1}^{\alpha_{\ast}}=\mathcal{D}^{\ell}_{\beta_{\ast}}([\alpha_{\ast}])

and

\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j+1}^{\alpha_{\ast}}=\{(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)},\mathfrak{I}^{(j+1)})\mid\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}},\ \mathfrak{I}^{(j+1)}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})-1}_{\beta_{\ast}}(\mathfrak{I}^{\infty})\}.

We define
\sigma\colon\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j+1}^{\alpha_{\ast}}\to\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
by

\sigma(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)},\mathfrak{I}^{(j+1)})=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})
.
When
\beta_{\ast}=1
, we set

\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}^{\alpha_{\ast}}_{j}=\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}^{\alpha_{\ast}}_{\beta_{\ast},j}
.

For
\boldsymbol{d}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}
and
\ell=0,1,\ldots,\alpha_{\ast}
, we
set
H_{\boldsymbol{d}}=\prod_{i=1}^{j}H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)
, and consider a product

M_{\boldsymbol{d}}=M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}|\beta)\times H_{\boldsymbol{d}}

We write by
\mathcal{V}^{(i)}
the tautological bundle

\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
on the component

H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)
.

For
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
, we set

Fl_{H_{\boldsymbol{d}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}})=\prod_{i=1}^{j}Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{\ast}^{(i)},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{i})

\widetilde{M}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}^{\ell}=\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\infty})\times Fl_{H_{\boldsymbol{d}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}})

where
\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{\ast}^{(k)}=\mathcal{V}_{\ast}^{(k)}/\mathcal{V}^{(k)}_{\infty^{\sharp}}
,

\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{i}=\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}\setminus\{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})\}
,
and write by
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{(i)}
the pull-back to

\widetilde{M}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}^{\ell}
of
the universal flag on each component

Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{\ast}^{(i)},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{i})
.
We regard
\{\mathcal{V}_{\ast(k)}\}_{k=1}^{\alpha_{\ast}}

on
\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\infty})

as a flag
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
of
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
, and
write by
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\infty}
the pull-back to

\widetilde{M}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}^{\ell}
.
For the tautological bundle
\mathcal{V}
on

\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta,\mathfrak{I}^{\infty})
,
we write by the same letter
the pull-backs to the product

\widetilde{M}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}^{\ell}
.
For
\mathcal{V}^{(i)}
, we also write by the same letter the
pull-back by the projection

\widetilde{M}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}^{\ell}\to H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)
.

We set
\mathbb{T}_{j}=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{t}\times\prod_{i=1}^{j}\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar_{i}}
.
We set
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{>i}=\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\infty}\oplus\bigoplus_{k>i}\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{(k)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{k}}
,

\mathcal{V}^{>i}=\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{k>i}\mathcal{V}^{(k)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{k}}
, and

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i}=\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{k>i}\mathfrak{I}^{(k)}
.
Here
e^{\hbar_{i}}
denotes the weight space

\mathbb{C}_{e^{\hbar_{i}}}
with the
weight
e^{\hbar_{i}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\hbar_{i}}
.

For
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\text{Dec}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
, we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})=

\displaystyle{1\over|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[F^{H}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F\left(\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{>0}_{\bullet}))\over\prod_{i=1}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i})\otimes\chi_{Q}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{F}^{>i}_{\bullet}))}

(38)

in
A^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{T}_{j}}(\widetilde{M}^{\ell}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta))
.
Here
\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i})\otimes\chi_{Q}
is a modification of
(
29
) by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp},\mathcal{V}^{\flat})\otimes\chi_{Q}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{h}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\gamma}}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{i})\right),

where the sum is taken for only cross terms

(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)=(\sharp,\flat),(\flat,\sharp)
.
In the numerator of (
38
), we have further
decomposition

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{>0}_{\bullet}))=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{\infty}_{\bullet}))\prod_{i=1}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet}))\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet},\mathcal{F}^{>i}_{\bullet})),

which is useful for recursive computations.

3.7
Recursions

For
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}
, we apply (
36
) to
\mathfrak{I}=\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}
, and

\ell=\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})-1
, and take
an equivariant cohomology class

\widetilde{C}=\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})
on
\mathcal{M}
.

When
j=0
, we
set
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=()
and
apply (
36
) to
\mathfrak{I}=\{1,\ldots,\alpha_{\ast}\}
and

\ell=\alpha_{\ast}
.

Then (
3.5
) is equal to

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar_{j+1}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\flat}|_{t}!\over|\mathfrak{I}|_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})-1}(\alpha-d\beta-d^{\sharp}\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}},\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})}(\mathcal{V}),

(39)

where
d^{\sharp}=|\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp}|/\beta_{\ast}
, and
(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}},\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)},\mathfrak{I}^{\sharp})\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}^{\alpha_{\ast}}_{\beta_{\ast},j+1}
.

Using
\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})
defined in (
38
),
we deduce recursion formula.

Lemma 3.3
.

For
l\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})-\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(-1)^{j}\oint d\hbar_{1}\cdots\oint d\hbar_{j}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}}\frac{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!}{[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{l}\oint d\hbar_{1}\cdots\oint d\hbar_{l}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},l}^{\alpha_{\ast}}}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{I}_{l})-1}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V}).

(40)

Proof.

We prove by induction on
j
.
For
l=1
, (
40
) is nothing but (
39
) for
j_{0}=0
and
\ell=n
.
For
l\geq 1
, we assume the formulas (
40
).
Then again by (
39
), the last summand for each
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},l}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
is equal to

\displaystyle{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\left(\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})\right.

\displaystyle-\oint d\hbar_{l+1}\left.\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{J}}\in\sigma^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}})}\frac{|\mathfrak{J}^{\infty}|_{t}!}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{J}^{(l+1)})-1}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{J}}}|\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{J}}}(\mathcal{V})\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{J}}=(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}},\mathfrak{J}^{(l+1)})
.
Hence we have (
40
) for general
l\geq 1
.
∎

For
l>\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}
, the set

\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},l}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
is empty.
Thus we get the following theorem,

which gives a proof of
Theorem
1.1

when
F(\mathcal{V})=\wedge_{-t}\Lambda^{\vee}
for some
\Lambda=\Lambda(\mathcal{V})
.

Theorem 3.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})-\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}(-1)^{j}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\oint d\hbar_{1}\cdots\oint d\hbar_{j}\int^{[K]}_{[\widetilde{M}^{0}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta)]^{vir}}\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})

(41)

where
\widetilde{C}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\mathcal{V})
is defined
in (
38
).

When
t=1
, we set

\displaystyle C_{\boldsymbol{d}}(\mathcal{V})=

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[H_{\boldsymbol{d}}]^{vir}}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F\left(\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\mathcal{V}_{\ast}^{(k)}/\mathcal{O}_{M_{\boldsymbol{d}}}\right)\over\prod_{i=1}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i})\otimes\chi_{Q}\right)}

(42)

for
\boldsymbol{d}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}^{j}_{>0}
.
Then we can simplify
(
41
) using the following lemma

Lemma 3.5
.

For
\boldsymbol{d}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}
with
|\boldsymbol{d}|\leq\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\left.\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}\ \right|\ \boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=\boldsymbol{d}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle={1\over\prod_{i=1}^{j}\lvert\boldsymbol{d}\rvert_{i}}{\alpha_{\ast}!\over|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|!\prod_{k=1}^{j}(|\mathfrak{I}^{(k)}|-1)!},

where we can take any

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})
with

\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=\boldsymbol{d}
in the right hand side,
and

\lvert\boldsymbol{d}\rvert_{i}=d^{(1)}+\cdots+d^{(i)}
and

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\rho^{-1}(\boldsymbol{d})
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
33
, Lemma 6.8]
since

|\mathfrak{I}_{k}|=d_{k}\beta_{\ast}
.
∎

Then we have

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})-\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}(-1)^{j}\sum_{\boldsymbol{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{j}_{>0}}{1\over\prod_{i=1}^{j}\lvert\boldsymbol{d}\rvert_{i}}\oint d\hbar_{1}\cdots\oint d\hbar_{j}\int^{[K]}_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}\rvert\beta)]^{vir}}C_{\boldsymbol{d}}(\mathcal{V}).

(43)

3.8
Euler class of the tangent bundle

For a
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant framed quiver
(Q,\chi_{Q})

and

Q_{0}
-graded vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
, we set

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})=\sum_{h\in Q_{1}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{h}}}-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},\mathcal{V}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\gamma}}}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{V}_{i}).

To evaluate (
41
), we use the following lemma.

Lemma 3.6
.

We have

\oint d\hbar\prod_{k=1}^{M}{1-e^{\hbar+b_{k}}\over 1-e^{\hbar+a_{k}}}\prod_{l=1}^{N}{1-e^{-\hbar+d_{l}}\over 1-e^{-\hbar+c_{l}}}=e^{\sum_{k=1}^{M}b_{k}-a_{k}}-e^{\sum_{l=1}^{N}d_{l}-c_{l}}.

Proof.

We can easily reduce to the case
where
N=0
.
To prove this case, we use multiplicative variables
u=e^{\hbar},z_{k}=e^{b_{k}}
and
w_{k}=e^{a_{k}}
for

k=1,\ldots,M
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar\prod_{k=1}^{M}\frac{1-e^{\hbar+b_{k}}}{1-e^{\hbar+a_{k}}}

\displaystyle=-\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{u=0,\infty}{du\over u}\prod_{k=1}^{M}{1-uz_{k}\over 1-uw_{k}}

\displaystyle=-1-\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{u=\infty}{du\over u}\prod_{k=1}^{M}{z_{k}\over w_{k}}{1-u^{-1}z_{k}^{-1}\over 1-u^{-1}w_{k}^{-1}}=-1+{z_{1}\cdots z_{M}\over w_{1}\cdots w_{M}}.

∎

In (
38
), we substitute

\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F\left(\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})\right)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\right)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\mathfrak{N}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i}\right)\right),

and divide the computations into three parts:

Lemma 3.7
.

For each
i=1,\ldots,j
, we have the following.

(1) We have

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar_{i}{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i})\otimes\chi_{Q}\right)\over\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{>i})\otimes\chi_{Q}\right)}=t^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(d^{(i)}\beta,\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta\right)}-t^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta,d^{(i)}\beta\right)},

where
the Euler form

\chi\left(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\right)
is defined
in (
1
)
.
We set

d^{\leq i}=\sum_{l\leq i}d^{(l)}
for

\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}=\left(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)}\right)={1\over\beta_{\ast}}\left(|\mathfrak{I}^{(1)}|,\ldots,|\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}|\right)
.

(2)

\int_{[Fl_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}^{(i)}_{0},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{i})]^{vir}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}((\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}))\right)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet}))={[d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1]_{t}!\over 1-t}\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})
, where

\displaystyle\gamma_{d^{(i)}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{[H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}\left(\Lambda_{Q^{\sharp}}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\infty^{\sharp}}\right)\right),

(44)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\infty^{\sharp}})}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\sum_{h\in Q_{1}\atop\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h),\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)\neq\infty}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{h}}+\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\infty^{\sharp}},\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\ast})}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\chi_{\gamma}}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{i}).}

(45)

(3)

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar_{i}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{F}^{>i}_{\bullet}))/\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{F}^{>i}_{\bullet}))=t^{s(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i})}-t^{s(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i},\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})}
,
where

\displaystyle s(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{I}^{\prime})=\left|\{(l,l^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{I}\times\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}\mid l<l^{\prime}\}\right|.

(46)

Proof.

(1)
For
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}=\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}^{\flat}=\mathcal{V}^{>i}
,
we see that

\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp},\mathcal{V}^{\flat})
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)}\left(\sum_{h\in Q_{1}\atop\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h),\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)\neq\infty}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})-\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{2}}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(\gamma)},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(\gamma)})-\sum_{v\in I}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\heartsuit}_{v},\mathcal{V}^{\spadesuit}_{v})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{h\in Q_{1}\atop\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)=\infty}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{\infty},\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)})+\sum_{h\in Q_{1}\atop\mathop{\mathrm{in}}\nolimits(h)=\infty}\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}_{\mathop{\mathrm{out}}\nolimits(h)},\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{\infty})

where the sum is taken for only cross terms
(\heartsuit,\spadesuit)=(\sharp,\flat),(\flat,\sharp)
.
By Lemma
3.6
, we do not need
\otimes\chi_{Q}
, and dimension countings give the assertion.
To prove (2) we remark that (
31
) implies

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\infty^{\sharp}},\mathcal{V}^{(i)}_{\ast})-\mathcal{O}_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)}\beta)}+\Theta(\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(i)}_{\bullet}),

and the right hand side does not include
e^{\hbar_{i}}
.
Together with (
45
),
the assertion follows from the integral

\int_{Fl(\mathbb{C}^{d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{(i)})}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(TFl(\mathbb{C}^{d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1},\bar{\mathfrak{I}}_{i}))=[d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1]_{t}!
.

Finally (3) follows from direct computations.
∎

By Lemma
3.7
, we compute (
41
),
and we have

\displaystyle\int_{M^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})-\int_{M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}}\frac{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!}{[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\prod_{i=1}^{j}\frac{[d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1]_{t}!}{t-1}\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})

\displaystyle\cdot(t^{s(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(d^{(i)}\beta,\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta\right)}}-t^{s(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i},\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta,d^{(i)}\beta\right)}})\int_{M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha-|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|\beta)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),

(47)

where
d^{(i)}=|\mathfrak{I}^{(i)}|/\beta_{\ast}
and

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i}=\bigsqcup_{k>i}\mathfrak{I}_{k}\sqcup\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}

for
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}=(\mathfrak{I}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathfrak{I}^{(j)})\in\text{Dec}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
,
and
\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})
are defined in
(
44
).

We set
\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}(\alpha_{\ast})=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}{}_{\beta_{\ast},j}^{\alpha_{\ast}}
and,
we get the following theorem.

Theorem 3.8

(Theorem
1.2
)
.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{[M^{\zeta^{+}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})-\int_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\alpha_{\ast}/\beta_{\ast}\rfloor}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}(\alpha_{\ast})\atop|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|=k}{|\mathfrak{I}^{\infty}|_{t}!\over[\alpha_{\ast}]_{t}!}\prod_{i=1}^{k}{[d^{(i)}\beta_{\ast}-1]_{t}!\over t-1}\gamma_{d^{(i)}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t})

\displaystyle\cdot(t^{s(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(d^{(i)}\beta,\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta\right)}}-t^{s(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i},\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\chi\left(\alpha-d^{\leq i}\beta,d^{(i)}\beta\right)}})\int_{[M^{\zeta^{-}}(\alpha-k\beta)]^{vir}}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}).

4
Affine Laumon space of type
A_{N-1}^{(1)}

We consider the framed quiver
Q^{N}\colon

and the associated framed quiver moduli spaces

M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
called
the
chainsaw quiver varieties

of type
A^{(1)}_{N-1}
where

\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{N})
and

\boldsymbol{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{N})
.
When
N=1
, the framed quiver moduli spaces
associated to
Q^{1}\colon

are called the
Jordan quiver varieties
.

We consider the group

\Gamma_{N}=\{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\mid\zeta^{N}=1\}
of
N
-th roots of the unit.

4.1
Affine Laumon space as a component of
\Gamma_{N}
-fixed points set

In the following, we idenfity

I=Q_{0}\setminus\{\infty\}
with

\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, and slightly change the notation
from the previous section.
Here we introduce
W=\bigoplus_{i\in I}W_{i}
with
W_{i}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(V_{\infty},V_{i})^{\oplus r_{i}}
corresponding to the framings to
i\in I
in
Q^{N}
, and set

V=\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
,
omitting
V_{\infty}
,
with
\mathop{\mathrm{dim}}\nolimits V_{i}=v_{i}
for
i\in I
.
Their shifts
W[l]
,
V[l]
have the gradings given by

W[l]_{i}=W_{i+l}
,
V[l]_{i}=V_{i+l}
.
They can be viewed as
\Gamma_{N}
-representations by regarding
gradings as weight
space decompositions.

We write by

\mathbb{C}_{\kappa/\zeta},\mathbb{C}_{q}
and

\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q/\zeta}
the weight spaces
of the group
\Gamma_{N}\times\mathbb{T}

with the weights
\kappa/\zeta
,
q
, and

\kappa q/\zeta
respectively,
where

\zeta\in\Gamma
, and

\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{q}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\kappa}\times\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{e_{\alpha}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\mu_{\alpha}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\nu_{\alpha}}
.

We set

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{Q^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}(W,V)=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V)\otimes\left(\mathbb{C}_{\kappa/\zeta}\oplus\mathbb{C}_{q}\right)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(W,V)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,W)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q/\zeta},

(48)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{L}_{Q^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}(V)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,V)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q/\zeta}.

(49)

We regard the component

\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{e_{\alpha}}

as the diagonal torus of
\operatorname{GL}(W_{1})\times\cdots\times\operatorname{GL}(W_{N})

naturally acting on
\mathbb{M}
,
and the other component
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\mu_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\mu_{r}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\nu_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{C}^{\ast}_{\nu_{r}}

acts on
\mathbb{M}
and
\mathbb{L}
trivially.
Then the
map

\mu=\mu_{Q^{1}}\colon\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{Q^{1}}\to\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{L}_{Q^{1}}
sending

(B_{1},B_{2},z,w)
to
B_{1}B_{2}-B_{2}B_{1}+zw
is

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant.
Thus we get the
\mathbb{T}
-action on the Jordan quiver varieties

M^{\pm}(r,v)=[\mu^{-1}(0)^{\pm}/\operatorname{GL}(V)]

where
r=r_{1}+\cdots+r_{N}
,
v=v_{1}+\cdots+v_{N}
, and

\mu^{-1}(0)^{\pm}
is the stable loci in
\mathbb{\mu}^{-1}(0)

with respect to the stability parameters
\pm 1
.
Elements in
\mu^{-1}(0)
are called

ADHM data
.

To define
\Gamma_{N}
-action on
M^{-}(r,v)
, we forget
the grading of
V
, and regard it as a just vector space
\mathbb{C}^{v}
.
Via the only
\Gamma_{N}
-action on
W
,
we have
\Gamma_{N}\times\operatorname{GL}(V)
-actions on
\mathbb{M}
,
\mathbb{L}
.
This induces
\Gamma_{N}
-action
on
M^{-}(r,v)
compatible with
\mathbb{T}
-action.

We take ADHM datum
(B,z,w)

representing
\Gamma_{N}
-fixed point in

M^{-}(r,v)
.
For
\zeta\in\Gamma_{N}
, there exists

g(\zeta)\in\operatorname{GL}(V)
such that

(\zeta,g(\zeta))\cdot(B,z,w)=(B,z,w)
.
Such an element
g(\zeta)
is uniquely determined by the stability for
(B,z,w)
.
This induces a group homomorphism

\Gamma_{N}\to\operatorname{GL}(V)

giving
I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
-grading on
V
.

Taking the degree
0
parts, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{Q^{N}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V,V[1])\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa}\times\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(W,V)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V,W[1])\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q},

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{Q^{N}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V,V[1])\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q}.

(50)

The
map

\mu\colon\mathbb{M}\to\mathbb{L}
keeps
these degree
0
parts and we have

\mu|_{\mathbb{M}_{Q^{N}}}=\mu_{Q^{N}}
.
For the stability parameters

\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}=(-1)_{i\in I},\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}=(1)_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
,
the associated framed quiver moduli spaces

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=\left[\mu_{Q^{N}}^{-1}(0)^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}}\Big/\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{GL}(V_{i})\right],\quad M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=\left[\mu_{Q^{N}}^{-1}(0)^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}}\Big/\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{GL}(V_{i})\right]

are called
the
affine Laumon space
of type
A^{(1)}_{N-1}
,
and the
dual
.
Here we notice that
\mu^{-1}(0)^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}}=\mu^{-1}(0)^{-}\cap\mathbb{M}_{Q^{N}}
and

\mu^{-1}(0)^{\zeta^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}}=\mu^{-1}(0)^{+}\cap\mathbb{M}_{Q^{N}}
.

We consider the
\Gamma_{N}
-fixed points set

M^{-}(r,v)^{\Gamma_{N}}
.
By the construction, we have

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant embeddings

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})\to M^{-}(r,v)^{\Gamma_{N}}
.
By
[
28
]
, all the connected components of

M^{-}(r,v)^{\Gamma_{N}}
are of this form taking all
possible
\boldsymbol{v}
such that
v_{1}+\cdots+v_{N}=v
.
In particular, the
\mathbb{T}
-fixed points sets

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
are
regarded as a subset of

M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}\times\Gamma_{N}}
.
In fact,
we see that

M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}}=\bigsqcup_{v_{1}+\cdots+v_{N}=v}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}},

and consequently
M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}}=M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}\times\Gamma_{N}}
.

This follows from combinatorial descriptions of

M^{-}(r,v)^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{T}}

summarized in the next subsection.

4.2
Combinatorial description
of
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{T}}

We recall combinatorial descriptions of
M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}}
,
the tautological bundle, and the
tangent bundles over the fixed points from
[
32
]
.

We can identify the all fixed points in

M^{-}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}}
with
the collections
\vec{Y}=(Y^{(1)},\ldots,Y^{(N)})
of Young diagrams

Y^{(1)},\ldots,Y^{(N)}
such that

|Y^{(1)}|+\cdots+|Y^{(N)}|=v
, where

|Y|
denotes the number of boxes in
Y

for a Young diagram
Y
.
By the gauge transformation, we have the
\mathbb{T}
-weight
decompositions

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r}\bigoplus_{(l,m)\in Y_{\alpha}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{-l+1}q^{-m+1}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}}

(51)

of tautological bundles

\mathcal{V}=[\mu^{-1}(0)^{-}\times V/\operatorname{GL}(V)]

and
\mathcal{W}=M^{-}(r,v)\times W
.
We define
B_{1},B_{2}\colon V\to V
,

\colon W\to V
, and
w=0\colon V\to W
so that

\displaystyle B_{1}(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{-l+1}q^{-m+1}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{-l}q^{-m+1}}&(l+1,m)\in Y_{\alpha}\\
0&(l+1,m)\not\in Y_{\alpha},\\
\end{cases}

(52)

\displaystyle B_{2}(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{-l+1}q^{-m+1}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{-l+1}q^{-m}}&(l,m+1)\in Y_{\alpha}\\
0&(l,m+1)\not\in Y_{\alpha},\\
\end{cases}

(53)

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}}},

\displaystyle w=0.

(54)

Then we have
(B_{1},B_{2},z,w)\in\mu^{-1}(0)^{-}
and
conjugations by the weight decompositions (
51
) gives
gauge transformations from the
\mathbb{T}
-orbits of

(B_{1},B_{2},z,w)
to itself.
Thus the equivalence class
[B_{1},B_{2},z,w]\in M^{-}(r,v)

is the
\mathbb{T}
-fixed point corresponding to
\vec{Y}
.

We identify a Young diagram
Y
with the partition

\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,)
where
\lambda_{i}
is the number
of boxes in
i
-th row in
Y
, and set
Y=Y_{\lambda}
.
For pairs
\lambda,\mu
of partitions,
we consider the
Nekrasov factor

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|N)}(u)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k|N)}(u|q,\kappa)=\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\lambda},Y_{\mu}}^{(k|N)}(u)=\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\lambda},Y_{\mu}}^{(k|N)}(u|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq l\leq l^{\prime}\atop-l+l^{\prime}\equiv k\mod N}(u\kappa^{-l+l^{\prime}}q^{-\mu_{l}+\lambda_{l^{\prime}+1}};q)_{\lambda_{l^{\prime}}-\lambda_{l^{\prime}+1}}\prod_{1\leq l\leq l^{\prime}\atop l-l^{\prime}-1\equiv k\mod N}(u\kappa^{l-l^{\prime}-1}q^{\lambda_{l}-\mu_{l^{\prime}}};q)_{\mu_{l^{\prime}}-\mu_{l^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{s\in Y_{\lambda}\atop L_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)\equiv k\mod N}(1-u\kappa^{L_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)}q^{-A_{Y_{\mu}}(s)-1})\prod_{t\in Y_{\mu}\atop-L_{Y_{\mu}}(t)-1\equiv k\mod N}(1-u\kappa^{-L_{Y_{\mu}}(t)-1}q^{A_{Y_{\lambda}}(t)}).

(55)

We often omit
N
and write
the Nekrasov factor (
55
)
by
\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\mu}^{(k)}(u)
, and so on.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\emptyset}^{(k)}(u)

\displaystyle=\prod_{(l,m)\in Y_{\lambda}\atop l-1\equiv k\mod N}(1-u\kappa^{l-1}q^{m-1}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\emptyset,\lambda}^{(k)}(u)

\displaystyle=\prod_{(l,m)\in Y_{\lambda}\atop-l\equiv k\mod N}(1-u\kappa^{-l}q^{-m}),

(56)

where
\emptyset
denotes the empty partition.
By comparing (
56
)
with
\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}
in (
51
),
we have

\wedge_{-1/\kappa q}\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{\emptyset,Y_{\alpha}}^{(0|1)}(e_{\alpha})

and

\wedge_{-1}\mathcal{V}^{\vee}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\alpha},\emptyset}^{(0|1)}(e_{\alpha}^{-1})
.
By
[
32
, Theorem 2.11]
, we have

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}T_{\vec{Y}}^{\ast}M^{-}(r,n)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\alpha},Y_{\beta}}^{(0|1)}(e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha}|q,\kappa).

(57)

For
\Gamma_{N}
-action, we rewrite

\boldsymbol{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r})
,

\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{r})
, and

\boldsymbol{\nu}=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{r})
by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{e}_{i})_{i\in I},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i})_{i\in I},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i})_{i\in I}

with

\boldsymbol{e}_{i}=(e_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}

so that

W_{i}=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{(i,\alpha)}}
,

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}=(\mu_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}
, and

\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i}=(\nu_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}
.
Set

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{deg}}\nolimits\kappa=-1,\quad\mathop{\mathrm{deg}}\nolimits q=0,\quad\mathop{\mathrm{deg}}\nolimits e_{(i,\alpha)}=i,

(58)

and

\mathop{\mathrm{deg}}\nolimits\mu_{(i,\alpha)}=\mathop{\mathrm{deg}}\nolimits\nu_{(i,\alpha)}=0\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}

for
i\in I
,
and
1\leq\alpha\leq r_{i}
.
We define a direct summand
V_{j}
of
V
by taking
the direct sum of components of
\mathcal{V}|_{\vec{Y}}
in (
51
)
with the weights of degree
j\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
.
Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle V_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l,m)\in Y_{(i,\alpha)}\\
i+l-1\equiv j\mod N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{(i,\alpha)}\kappa^{-l+1}q^{-m+1}}.

(59)

Then we have
(B_{1},B_{2},z,w)\in\mathbb{M}_{Q^{N}}
, and

[B_{1},B_{2},z,w]\in M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
.
Thus we get the proposition.

Proposition 4.1
.

(a) We have a bijection from the fixed points set

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
to the set of collections

\vec{Y}=(Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{i,\alpha}
of Young diagrams

Y_{(i,\alpha)}
indexed by
i\in I
, and

1\leq\alpha\leq r_{i}
such that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\left|\left\{\left.(l,m)\in Y_{(i,\alpha)}\ \right|\ i+l-1\equiv j\mod N\right\}\right|=v_{j}.

The corresponding
Q^{N}
-representation at each fixed point
\vec{Y}
is
described by
(
51
), (
52
), (
53
), and (
54
).

(b) For the fixed point
\vec{Y}\in M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}

via the bijection in (a), we have

\displaystyle\wedge_{-\kappa q}\mathcal{V}_{i}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{N}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathsf{N}_{\emptyset,Y_{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i-1)}(e_{(j,\beta)}),

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\vee}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},\emptyset}^{(j-i)}(e_{(i,\alpha)}^{-1}).

(60)

(c) For the fixed point
\vec{Y}\in M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}

via the bijection in (a), we have

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}T^{\ast}_{\vec{Y}}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},Y_{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i|N)}(e_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa).

(61)

Proof.

(a) follows from the preceding arguments.
(b) follows by comparing (
59
) and (
56
).
For (c), notice that the tangent space

T_{\vec{Y}}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

at the fixed point
\vec{Y}=(Y_{(i,\alpha)})

is isomorphic to the degree
0

part of
T_{\vec{Y}}M^{-}(r,v)
.
We compute the weight space decomposition of

T_{\vec{Y}}M^{-}(r,v)=\mathbb{M}-\mathbb{L}-\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V)

in the representation ring
K_{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})
, where

V
is identified with
\mathcal{V}|_{\vec{Y}}
in (
51
).
Then
\Gamma_{N}
-action defined in (
49
) is compatible
with the degree (
58
).
Hence we have the assertion.
∎

4.3
Relations between
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
and
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

For combinatorial descriptions of
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
,
we consider a map
\mathbb{M}(W,V)\to\mathbb{M}(W^{\vee},V^{\vee})

sending
(B_{1},B_{2},z,w)
to

(-B_{1}^{\vee},B_{2}^{\vee},w^{\vee},z^{\vee})
where

∨
denotes taking the transpose

f^{\vee}\colon U_{2}^{\vee}\to U_{1}^{\vee}
for a
linear map
f\colon U_{1}\to U_{2}
.
By the remark after Definition
2.1
, this map
interchanges stability conditions
with respect to the stability parameters

\pm 1\in\mathbb{R}
.
Hence it induces a map
\mu^{-1}(0)^{+}\to\mu^{-1}(0)^{-}
.
To make this map

\operatorname{GL}(V)\times\mathbb{T}
-equivariant,
we take a group homomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{GL}(V)\times\mathbb{T}\to\operatorname{GL}(V^{\vee})\times\mathbb{T},\quad(g,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu},\kappa,q)\mapsto(\kappa q\ (g^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\vee})^{-1},\kappa,q),

(62)

where we identify the diagonal
torus in
\operatorname{GL}(W)
with the one in
\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})
by taking
the dual base.

For a
I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector space
U
,
we define the grading of the dual space
U^{\vee}

by
(U^{\vee})_{i}=(U_{-i})^{\vee}
.
Then the above map induces an isomorphism

\Phi\colon M^{+}(r,v)\to M^{-}(r,v)
respecting
sub-manifolds
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
and

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])
such that

\Phi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j}=\mathcal{V}_{-j-1}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q}

for
j\in I
, and
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant after taking
a homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T},\quad(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu})\mapsto((\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\vee})^{-1}).

(63)

Here we set
(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee})_{i}=r_{-i}
,

(\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])_{i}=(\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee})_{i+1}=v_{-i-1}
, and

((\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1})_{i}=((\boldsymbol{e}_{-i}^{\vee})^{-1})\in GL(W_{-i}^{\vee})
for
i\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence we have
the combinatorial descriptions
similar to the previous sub-section.

We identify
M^{+}(r,v)^{\mathbb{T}}
with the set of
the collections
\vec{Y}=(Y^{(1)},\ldots,Y^{(N)})
of Young diagrams

Y^{(1)},\ldots,Y^{(N)}
such that

|Y^{(1)}|+\cdots+|Y^{(N)}|=v
.
We have the
\mathbb{T}
-weight decompositions

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r}\bigoplus_{(l,m)\in Y_{\alpha}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l}q^{m}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}}

(64)

of tautological bundles

\mathcal{V}=[\mu^{-1}(0)^{+}\times V/\operatorname{GL}(V)]

and
\mathcal{W}=M^{+}(r,v)\times W
.
We define
B_{1},B_{2}\colon V\to V
,

z\colon W\to V
, and
w\colon V\to W

so that

\displaystyle B_{1}(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l}q^{m}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l-1}q^{m}}&(l-1,m)\in Y_{\alpha}\\
0&(l-1,m)\not\in Y_{\alpha},\\
\end{cases}

(65)

\displaystyle B_{2}(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l}q^{m}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l}q^{m-1}}&(l,m-1)\in Y_{\alpha}\\
0&(l,m-1)\not\in Y_{\alpha},\\
\end{cases}

(66)

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle w(e_{\alpha}\kappa^{l}q^{m})=\begin{cases}e_{\alpha}&l=m=1\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(67)

Then the equivalence class
[B_{1},B_{2},z,w]\in M^{+}(r,v)

is the
\mathbb{T}
-fixed point corresponding to
\vec{Y}
.

Comparing (
56
)
with
\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}
in (

),
we have

\wedge_{-1/\kappa q}\mathcal{V}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{\emptyset,Y_{\alpha}}^{(0|1)}(e_{\alpha})

and

\wedge_{-1}\mathcal{V}^{\vee}\Big|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\alpha},\emptyset}^{(0|1)}(e_{\alpha}^{-1})
.
By applying
\Phi^{\ast}
to (
57
) and
taking the homomorphism (
63
) of
\mathbb{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}T_{\vec{Y}}^{\ast}M^{+}(r,n)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{r}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{\alpha},Y_{\beta}}^{(0|1)}(e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta}|q,\kappa).

(68)

On
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
, we have

\displaystyle V_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l,m)\in Y_{(i,\alpha)}\\
i-l\equiv j\mod N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{(i,\alpha)}\kappa^{l}q^{m}},

(69)

and the similar proposition
for
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
to Proposition
4.1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

(a) We have a bijection from the fixed point set

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
to the set of collections

\vec{Y}=(Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{i,\alpha}
of Young diagrams

, and

such that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\left|\left\{(l,m)\in Y_{(i,\alpha)}\mid i-l\equiv j\mod N\right\}\right|=v_{j}.

The corresponding

is
described by
(

), (

), (
66
), and (
67

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\vee}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\mathsf{N}_{\emptyset,Y_{(i,\alpha)}}^{(j-i)}(e_{(i,\alpha)}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\wedge_{-\kappa q}\mathcal{V}_{j}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{(i,\alpha),\emptyset}}^{(i-j-1)}(e_{(i,\alpha)}).

(70)

(c) For the fixed point
\vec{Y}\in M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

via the bijection in (a),

\displaystyle\wedge_{-1}T^{\ast}_{\vec{Y}}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathsf{N}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},Y_{(j,\beta)}}^{(i-j|N)}(e_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa).

(71)

The tangent space
T_{\vec{Y}}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

at the fixed point
\vec{Y}=(Y_{(i,\alpha)})

via the bijection in (a) is isomorphic to the degree
0

part of
T_{\vec{Y}}M^{+}(r,v)
.

Proof.

(a) follows from (
69
).
By comparing it with (

), we have (b).
By taking degree 0 part in (
