), we have (c).
∎

From the combinatorial point of view,
we introduce stable and co-stable collections

\boldsymbol{Y}^{\pm}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\left((Y^{\pm}_{i,\alpha})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
of colored Young
diagrams associated to
a collection

\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\left((\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}

of partitions.
We define
stable collections
\boldsymbol{Y}^{-}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\left((Y^{-}_{i,\alpha})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}

of colored Young
diagrams
by labeling boxes in
k
-th row a color
i+k-1\in I

in
i
-th Young diagrams
(Y_{i,1},\ldots,Y_{i,r_{i}})
.
On the other hand, we define
co-stable collections
\boldsymbol{Y}^{+}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\left((Y^{+}_{i,\alpha})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}

of colored Young
diagrams
by labeling boxes in
k
-th row a color
i-k\in I

in
i
-th Young diagrams
(Y_{i,1},\ldots,Y_{i,r_{i}})
:

We set
Y^{\pm}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\bigsqcup_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}Y_{i,\alpha}
.
Then
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
is parametrized by
the collections
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\left((\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
of partitions such that

\sharp\{s\in Y^{-}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mid s\text{ has a color }i\in I\}=v_{i}

for all
i\in I
.

M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
is parametrized by
the collections
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\left((\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
of partitions such that

\sharp\{s\in Y^{+}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mid s\text{ has a color }i\in I\}=v_{i}

for all
i\in I
.

4.4
Non-stationary Ruijsenaars function

We define the
K
-theoretic class

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in I}\sum_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathcal{V}_{j}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{1/e_{(j,\beta)}}+\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i+1}}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{e_{(i+1,\alpha)}\kappa q}

\displaystyle+\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V,V[1])\otimes(\mathbb{C}_{\kappa}-\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q})+\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{N}}(V)\otimes(\mathbb{C}_{q}-\mathbb{C}),

(72)

using tautological bundles on moduli stacks.
By the construction using (
50
),
we have
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=TM^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

in
K_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v}))
.

Proposition 4.3
.

(a)
For
\vec{Y}=((Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}})_{i\in I}\in M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
,
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(te_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa).

(b)
For
\vec{Y}=((Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}})_{i\in I}\in M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
,
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathsf{N}^{(i-j|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(te_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa).

For variables
\boldsymbol{p}=(p_{i})_{i\in I}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{N})
,
set

Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits},\quad\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}.

Proposition 4.4
.

(a) We have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{r_{i}}}\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(te_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(e_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa)}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{j\geq 1}p_{i+j-1}^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}},

where
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\left((\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
runs in

\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{r_{i}}
for
the set
\mathsf{P}
of partitions.

(b) We have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{r_{i}}}\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}^{(i-j|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(te_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}^{(i-j|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(e_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa)}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{j\geq 1}p_{i-j}^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}}

\displaystyle=Z^{\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}((\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1},t|(\boldsymbol{p}[-1])^{\vee}|q,\kappa).

Proof.

These follows from Proposition
4.1
and
Proposition
4.2
.
We can also check the second equality in (b) from

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle=\Phi^{\ast}\left.T^{\ast}M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])\right|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{e}=(\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1}\end{subarray}}.

In fact, the fixed points set
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])^{\boldsymbol{T}}

is parametrized by
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\vee}=(\lambda^{(-i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}})_{i\in I}

for
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}P^{r_{i}}
, and we have

\displaystyle\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(-i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(-j,\beta)}}(te_{(-j,\beta)}^{-1}/e_{(-i,\alpha)}^{-1}|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(-i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(-j,\beta)}}(e_{(-j,\beta)}^{-1}/e_{(-i,\alpha)}^{-1}|q,\kappa)}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{j\geq 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}(\boldsymbol{p}[-1]^{\vee})^{\lambda^{(-i,\alpha)}_{j}}_{i+j-1}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}^{(-j+i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(te_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}^{(-j+i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(e_{(i,\alpha)}/e_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa)}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{j\geq 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}(\boldsymbol{p}[-1]^{\vee})^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}}_{-i+j-1}.

∎

When
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=d
for a positive integer

d
,
following
[
42
]
, we introduce
the
non-stationary Ruijsenaars function

\displaystyle f^{\hat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{N}}(\boldsymbol{x},p|\boldsymbol{s},\kappa|q,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{d}}\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}{{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(ts_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)})\over\mathsf{N}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i|N)}(s_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)})}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{j\geq 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{d}(px_{i+j}/t^{d}x_{i+j-1})^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}},

where
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{i})_{i\in I}
are variables,

\boldsymbol{s}=((s_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{d})_{i\in I}
,

p,q,\kappa
,
and
t

are parameters.
This is slightly generalized notion, which is more
suitable here, without assuming

r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=1
.
It is easy to see

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{s},t|(px_{i+1}/t^{d}x_{i})_{i\in I}|q,\kappa)=f^{\hat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{N}}(\boldsymbol{x},p|\boldsymbol{s},\kappa|q,t).

(73)

For a positive integer
d
, set

\displaystyle f_{N,\infty}(X|S|q,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\theta\in(\widehat{M}_{N})^{d}}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}c_{N,\infty}(\theta^{(\alpha)},\theta^{(\beta)}|S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}|q,t)\prod_{1\leq i<k\leq N}(X_{k}/X_{i})^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}

with
\widehat{M}_{N}
the set of infinite,
N
-periodic, strictly upper
triangular matrices with non-negative integer entries
which are non-zero only in a finite strip away from the
diagonal:

(\widehat{M}_{N})^{d}=\{\theta=(\theta^{(\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{d}\mid\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}=\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i+N,k+N}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\ (i,k\geq 1,\alpha=1,\ldots,d),\ \theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}=0\ (k\leq i,k\gg i)\},

where we set

\theta^{(\alpha)}=(\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik})_{i,k=1}^{\infty}
for
\alpha=1,\ldots,d
.

Here
X=(X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,)
and
S=(S_{\alpha})_{\alpha=1}^{d}
with

S_{\alpha}=(S_{(1,\alpha)},S_{(2,\alpha)},\ldots,)
are

d+1
types of infinite variables, and

\displaystyle c_{N,\infty}(\theta^{(\alpha)},\theta^{(\beta)}|S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}|q,t)=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{i<j\leq k<\infty}{(q^{\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}tS_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}\over(q^{\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}S_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{i\leq j<k<\infty}{(q^{-\theta^{(\beta)}_{jk}+\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}qS_{(j,\beta)}/tS_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}\over(q^{-\theta^{(\beta)}_{jk}+\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}S_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}}.

We substitute
\bar{x}=(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\ldots,),\ \bar{s}=\left(\bar{s}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha=1}^{d}
with

\bar{s}_{\alpha}=(\bar{s}_{(1,\alpha)},\bar{s}_{(2,\alpha)},\ldots,)
such that

\bar{x}_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,\bar{x}_{N}=x_{N},\text{ and }\bar{x}_{i+kN}=p^{k}x_{i}\ (i=1\ldots,N,k=1,2,\ldots,)

\bar{s}_{(1,\alpha)}=s_{(1,\alpha)},\ldots,\bar{s}_{(N,\alpha)}=s_{(N,\alpha)},\text{ and }\bar{s}_{(i+kN,\alpha)}=\kappa^{k}s_{(i,\alpha)}\ (i=1\ldots,N,k=1,2,\ldots,).

We set
\delta=(N-1,N-2,\ldots,1,0)\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
.
By
[
24
, Theorem 3.2]
, we have the proposition.

Proposition 4.5
.

When
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=d
, we have

f^{\hat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{N}}(p^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{x},p^{1/N}|\kappa^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{s},\kappa^{1/N}|q,q/t)=f_{N,\infty}(\bar{x}|\bar{s}|q,t).

Proof.

This is a straight forward generalization of the
arguments in
the proof of
[
24
, Theorem 3.2]
.
In fact, we set

\displaystyle C_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}|q,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{i<j\leq k<\infty}{(q^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{k-j+1}}tS_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}\over(q^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{k-j+1}}qS_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{i\leq j<k<\infty}{(q^{-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{k-j}+\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}qS_{(j,\beta)}/tS_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}\over(q^{-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{k-j+1}+\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}S_{(j,\beta)}/S_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k-i+1}}}

where we set
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)}=(\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j})
and

\lambda^{(i+N,\alpha)}_{j}=\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}

for
\alpha=1,\ldots,d
.
Relations
\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ij}=\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j-i}
and
\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k}=\sum_{j\geq i+k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ij}
gives a
bijection of
(\widehat{M})^{d}
and

\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{d}
.
Then by
[
24
, Lemma 3.5]
, we have

f_{N,\infty}(\bar{x}|\bar{s}|q,t)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{d}}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}C_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|\bar{s}_{\alpha},\bar{s}_{\beta}|q,t)\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{k\geq 1}(\bar{x}_{i+k}/\bar{x}_{i+k-1})^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k}}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle f^{\hat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{N}}(p^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{x},p^{1/N}|\kappa^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{s},\kappa^{1/N}|q,q/t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{d}}(t/q)^{d\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)}|}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\widetilde{C}_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|s_{\alpha},s_{\beta}|q,t,\kappa)

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{k\geq 1}(\bar{x}_{i+k}/\bar{x}_{i+k-1})^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k}},

where
|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)}|=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\geq 1}\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j}
,

s_{\alpha}=(s_{(1,\alpha)},\ldots,s_{(N,\alpha)})

for
\alpha=1,\ldots,d
, and

\widetilde{C}_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|s_{\alpha},s_{\beta}|q,t,\kappa)=\prod_{i,j=1}^{N}{{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}((q/t)(\kappa^{\delta/N}s_{\beta})_{j}/(\kappa^{\delta/N}s_{\alpha})_{i}|q,\kappa^{1/N})\over\mathsf{N}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i|N)}((\kappa^{\delta/N}s_{\beta})_{j}/(\kappa^{\delta/N}s_{\alpha})_{i}|q,\kappa^{1/N})}.

By the similar argument as in
[
24
, Appendix A]
,
we have

(t/q)^{|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\beta}|}\cdot\widetilde{C}_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|s_{\alpha},s_{\beta}|q,t,\kappa)=C_{N,\infty}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\alpha)},\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(\beta)}|\bar{s}_{\alpha},\bar{s}_{\beta}|q,t).

This completes the proof.
∎

Conjecture 4.6
.

(1)
For
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}=(\delta_{il})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
we have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}{(qp_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(tp_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(qtp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(\kappa^{N}tp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(t^{2}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(q\kappa^{N}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}},

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}{(qp_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(tp_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(qtp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(\kappa^{N}tp_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(t^{2}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}(q\kappa^{N}p_{1}\cdots p_{N};q,\kappa^{N},tp_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}.

(2)
When
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=d
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa).

When
N=1
, Conjecture
4.6
is already
checked in

[
31
]
and later in
[
15
]
.
See also
[
11
]
for cohomological
version.

Proposition 4.7
.

When
N=1
, we have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=Z^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)={(qtp_{1};q,\kappa,tp_{1})_{\infty}(\kappa tp_{1};q,\kappa,tp_{1})_{\infty}\over(t^{2}p_{1};q,\kappa,tp_{1})_{\infty}(q\kappa p_{1};q,\kappa,tp_{1})_{\infty}}.

Proof.

It follows from
[
15
, Theorem 3.41]
after
\kappa\leftrightarrow t^{-1}
.
∎

Theorem 4.8
.

We assume Conjecture
4.6
.
Then for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)}=

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}\prod_{l\in I}{(qt^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{(t^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(qt^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

(74)

where
\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert=r_{1}+\cdots+r_{N}
.

4.5
Proof of Theorem
4.8

For a Young diagram
Y=Y_{\lambda}
and
k\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, set

\displaystyle\lvert Y\rvert_{k}

\displaystyle=\lvert\lambda\rvert_{k}=\lvert\{s=(i,j)\in Y\mid i\equiv k\mod N\}\rvert,

\displaystyle l_{Y}(k)

\displaystyle=l_{\lambda}(k)=\lvert\{s\in Y\mid L_{Y}(s)\equiv k\mod N\}\rvert.

Lemma 4.9
.

For
k=0,1,\ldots,N-1
, we have
\lvert Y\rvert_{k+1}=l_{Y}(k)
.

Proof.

We consider the one column
Y
of
L=lN+m
boxes
with
0\leq m<N
.
We have

\displaystyle\lvert Y\rvert_{k+1}=\begin{cases}l&m\leq k\\
l+1&m\geq k+1.\end{cases}

(75)

On the other hand, we have
l_{Y}(k)=\lvert\{s=(i,j)\in Y\mid L-i\equiv k\mod N\}\rvert
.
So we label numbers to boxes in the
one column
Y

from the bottom beginning with zero.
Then we have the same formula (
75
) for

l_{Y}(k)
.
For general
Y
, we can check the assertion by
repeating the similar procedures to each column.
∎

For
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}{\sf P}^{r_{i}}
with

\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}=(\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}
and
k\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, set

\displaystyle\lvert\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}\rvert_{k}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\lvert\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}\rvert_{k}

for each
i\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
.
For
\boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{N})\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, set

t^{\boldsymbol{m}}=(t^{m_{1}},\ldots,t^{m_{N}})
.

Proposition 4.10
.

For
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{i})_{i\in I}
and

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}=(\delta_{il})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
for
l\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}\to\infty}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|t^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)\cdot Z^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|t^{\boldsymbol{r}[1]}\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa),

\displaystyle\lim_{e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}\to\infty}\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|t^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)\cdot\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|t^{\boldsymbol{r}[1]}\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa),

where
\boldsymbol{e}=(\boldsymbol{e}_{i})_{i\in I}
and

\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}}=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}}_{i})_{i\in I}
with

\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}}_{l}=(e_{(l,1)},\ldots,e_{(l,r_{l})},e_{(l,r_{l}+1)})
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{e}}_{i}=\boldsymbol{e}_{i}

for
i\neq l
.

Proof.

For each
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}{\sf P}^{r_{i}+\delta_{il}}
,
the contribution of the factors

\prod_{i\neq l}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}{{\sf N}^{(l-i)}_{\lambda_{(i,\alpha)},\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(te_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})\over{\sf N}^{(l-i)}_{\lambda_{(i,\alpha)},\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})}\cdot\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}{{\sf N}^{(0)}_{\lambda_{(l,\alpha)},\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(te_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(l,\alpha)})\over{\sf N}^{(0)}_{\lambda_{(l,\alpha)},\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(N,\alpha)})}

is a multiplication of power for
t
under taking the
limit

e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}\to\infty
.
By (
55
) and Lemma (
4.9
),

the number of power in

t
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\left(l_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}}(l-i)+l_{\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(i-l-1)\right)+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\left(l_{\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}}(0)+l_{\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq l}\lvert\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}\rvert_{l-i+1}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{1}+\sum_{k\neq l}r_{k}\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{k-l}+r_{l}\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{0}.

On the other hand, the contribution of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}

to power of
\boldsymbol{p}
in
Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
is equal to

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{k-i+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\sum_{i\neq l}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{k-i+1}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{k-l+1}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{k-l+1}}.

Combining both contributions, we get

(tp_{l})^{\sum_{i\neq l}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{l-i+1}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{1}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{N}(t^{r_{k+1}}p_{k})^{\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{k-l+1}},

and hence the assertion for
Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
.

For
\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
, we consider power in
t

from the contribution of the factors

\prod_{i\neq l}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}{{\sf N}^{(l-i)}_{\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)},\lambda_{(i,\alpha)}}(te_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})\over{\sf N}^{(l-i)}_{\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)},\lambda_{(i,\alpha)}}(e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})}\cdot\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}{{\sf N}^{(0)}_{\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)},\lambda_{(l,\alpha)}}(te_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(l,\alpha)})\over{\sf N}^{(0)}_{\lambda_{(l,r_{l}+1)},\lambda_{(l,\alpha)}}(e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}/e_{(l,\alpha)})}.

By (
55
) and Lemma (
4.9
),
the number of power in
t
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq l}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}l_{\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(l-i)+l_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}}(i-l-1)+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}l_{\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}}(0)+l_{\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}}(-1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq l}\lvert\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}\rvert_{i-l}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{0}+\sum_{k\neq l}r_{k}\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{l-k+1}+r_{l}\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{1}.

On the other hand, the contribution of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}

to power of
\boldsymbol{p}
in
Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
is equal to

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{i-k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\sum_{i\neq l}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{i-k}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{l-k}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{N}p_{k}^{\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{l-k}}.

Combining both contributions, we get

(tp_{l})^{\sum_{i\neq l}|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{(i)}|_{i-l}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{l}}\lvert\lambda^{(l,\alpha)}\rvert_{0}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{N}(t^{r_{k}}p_{k-1})^{\lvert\lambda^{(l,r_{l}+1)}\rvert_{l-k+1}},

and hence the assertion for
\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
.
∎

Combining Conjecture
4.6
and Proposition
4.10
,
we give a proof of Theorem
4.8
.
For
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we write by
\Phi^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{p})

the right hand side of (
74
), that is, set

\displaystyle\Phi^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{p})

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}\prod_{l\in I}{(qt^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(t^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}+1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}\over(qt^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l+k-1}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N},t)_{\infty}}

We take large enough positive integer
d
such that

d\geq r_{l}

for any
l\in I
.
By Conjecture
4.6
(2), we have

\widecheck{Z}^{\sum_{l\in I}d\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=Z^{\sum_{l\in I}d\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
which is
equivalent to
the equation (
74
).
By the induction on
\sum_{l\in I}(d-r_{l})
,
we can assume
(
74
) holds for
\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}/Z^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
for some

l\in I
.
Applying Proposition
4.10
for the limit

e_{(l,r_{l}+1)}\to\infty
and
substituting
p_{l}=t^{-1}p_{l}
, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle\Phi^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}(t^{-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p}){Z^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(t^{\boldsymbol{r}[1]-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p})\over\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(t^{\boldsymbol{r}[1]-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}(t^{-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l}}\boldsymbol{p})\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}{(qt^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l-k+1}}p_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{l}+\cdots+r_{l-k+1}+1}p_{l-1}\cdots p_{l-k};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(t^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(qt^{r_{l+1}+\cdots+r_{l+k}-1}p_{l}\cdots p_{l+k-1};q,t^{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{p}).

This gives a proof of Theorem
4.8
.

4.6
Examples of Conjecture
4.6

Here we assume Conjecture
4.6
.
For
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, we set

F_{\ell}(x)={(qx;q,t^{\ell}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}\over(tx;q,t^{\ell}p_{1}\cdots p_{N})_{\infty}}.

Then Theorem
4.8
implies the following
expressions.

When
N=2
, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(r_{1},r_{2})}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}{F_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{1})\over F_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{1})}{F_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{2})\over F_{r_{1}+r_{2}}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{2})}.

For example, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle{F_{3}(tp_{1})\over F_{3}(tp_{2})}.

When
N
=3, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{1})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{1})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+n}p_{2})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{2})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{3})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+n}p_{3})}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{1}p_{2})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{3}+n}p_{2}p_{3})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{2}p_{3})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{3}p_{1})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{3}+n}p_{3}p_{1})}.

For example, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(2,2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(2,2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle{F_{5}(tp_{2})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{5}(tp_{3})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{1}p_{3})},

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(2,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(2,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle{F_{4}(tp_{1})F_{4}(t^{2}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{4}(tp_{3})F_{4}(t^{2}p_{2}p_{3})},

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(3,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(3,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle{F_{5}(tp_{1})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{1})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{1}p_{2})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{5}(tp_{3})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{3})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{2}p_{3})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{2}p_{3})},

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(3,2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})\over Z^{(3,2,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{p})}=

\displaystyle{F_{6}(tp_{2})F_{6}(t^{2}p_{1})F_{6}(t^{3}p_{1}p_{2})F_{6}(t^{4}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{6}(tp_{3})F_{6}(t^{2}p_{3})F_{6}(t^{4}p_{1}p_{3})F_{6}(t^{3}p_{2}p_{3})}.

When
N=4
, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}}=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{1})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{1})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+n}p_{2})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+n}p_{2})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+n}p_{3})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+n}p_{3})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+n}p_{4})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+n}p_{4})}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{1}p_{2})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+r_{4}+n}p_{2}p_{3})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{2}p_{3})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+r_{1}+n}p_{3}p_{4})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+r_{4}+n}p_{3}p_{4})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{4}p_{1})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+r_{1}+n}p_{4}p_{1})}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4}+n}p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+r_{4}+r_{1}+n}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4}+n}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4})}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{3}p_{4}p_{1})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{3}+r_{4}+r_{1}+n}p_{3}p_{4}p_{1})}{F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+n}p_{4}p_{1}p_{2})\over F_{\lvert\boldsymbol{r}\rvert}(t^{r_{4}+r_{1}+r_{2}+n}p_{4}p_{1}p_{2})}.

For example, we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{(2,1,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}\over Z^{(2,1,1,1)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}}=

\displaystyle{F_{5}(tp_{1})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{1}p_{2})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})\over F_{5}(tp_{4})F_{5}(t^{2}p_{3}p_{4})F_{5}(t^{3}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4})}.

4.7
Affine Laumon function

We define
K
-theoretic class
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}

on
M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})

for a generic stability parameter
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{I}

by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}=\sum_{j\in I}\sum_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}\mathcal{V}_{j}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{1/\mu_{(j,\beta)}}+\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i+1}}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\nu_{(i+1,\alpha)}\kappa q}.

(76)

This class is defined on any moduli stacks with
\mathbb{T}
-action
and tautological bundles
\mathcal{V}_{i}
for
i\in I
.

By (
60
) and (
70
),
we have the proposition.

Proposition 4.11
.

(a)
For
\vec{Y}=((Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}})_{i\in I}\in M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
,
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},\emptyset}(\mu_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})\cdot{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\emptyset,Y_{(j,\beta)}}(e_{(j,\beta)}/\nu_{(i,\alpha)}).

(b)
For
\vec{Y}=((Y_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}})_{i\in I}\in M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})^{\mathbb{T}}
,
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})|_{\vec{Y}}=\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\emptyset,Y_{(i,\alpha)}}(\mu_{(j,\beta)}/e_{(i,\alpha)})\cdot{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{Y_{(j,\beta)},\emptyset}(e_{(j,\beta)}/\nu_{(i,\alpha)}).

Set

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{N}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits},

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{N}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}.

Proposition 4.12
.

We have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)=Z^{\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}((\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\vee})^{-1},(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\vee})^{-1}|(\boldsymbol{p}[-1])^{\vee}|q,\kappa).

Proof.

We recall that the isomorphism
\Phi\colon M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})\cong M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])
satisfies

\Phi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{-i-1}\cong\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\kappa q}
, and is
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
after taking the homomorphism (
63
) of
\mathbb{T}
.
Applying these properties to
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}\in K_{\mathbb{T}}(M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1]))
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi^{\ast}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle=\Phi^{\ast}\left(\sum_{j\in I}\sum_{\beta=1}^{r_{-j-1}}\mathcal{V}_{-j-1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1/\mu_{(-j-1,\beta)}}+\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{-i}}\mathcal{V}_{-i-1}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu_{(-i,\alpha)}/\kappa q}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in I}\sum_{\beta=1}^{r^{\vee}_{j+1}}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu_{(j+1,\beta)}/\kappa q}+\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r^{\vee}_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{i}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1/\mu_{(i,\alpha)}}=\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}\in K(M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})).

Hence we have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{\boldsymbol{v}}\left.\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v}^{\vee}[1])}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}\right|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{e}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{\nu}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{-1}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\boldsymbol{p}^{(\boldsymbol{v}[-1])^{\vee}}\left.\int_{M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r}^{\vee},\boldsymbol{v})}\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}\right|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{e}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{\nu}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}={}^{t}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{-1}\end{subarray}},

and the assertion follows.
∎

For parameters

\boldsymbol{a}=\left((a_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
,

\boldsymbol{b}=\left((b_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
,
and
\boldsymbol{c}=\left((c_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
, and
variables
\boldsymbol{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{N})
, set

\displaystyle f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{a}\\
\boldsymbol{b}\\
\boldsymbol{c}\end{array}\right|\boldsymbol{p}\right|q,\kappa\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{r_{j}}{{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\emptyset,Y_{(j,\beta)}}(a_{(i,\alpha)}/b_{(j,\beta)})\cdot{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},\emptyset}(b_{(i,\alpha)}/c_{(j,\beta)})\over\mathsf{N}_{Y_{(i,\alpha)},Y_{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i|N)}(b_{(i,\alpha)}/b_{(j,\beta)}|q,\kappa)}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i,j\in I}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}p_{j}^{\sum|\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}|_{j-i+1}},

(80)

where
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\left((\lambda_{(i,\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{r_{i}}\right)_{i\in I}
runs in

\prod_{i\in I}\mathsf{P}^{r_{i}}
for
the set
\mathsf{P}
of partitions, and

\emptyset
denotes the empty partition.
For a partition
\lambda
, we set

|\lambda|_{k}=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{k+lN}.

By Proposition
4.11
, we have the proposition.

Proposition 4.13
.

(a) We have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\nu}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{-1}\end{array}\right|\boldsymbol{p}\right|q,\kappa\right).

(b) We have

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\vee}\\
\boldsymbol{e}^{\vee}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}^{\vee}\end{array}\right|(\boldsymbol{p}[-1])^{\vee}\right|q,\kappa\right).

Proof.

We have to prove only (b).
It follows from (a) and Proposition
4.12
.
∎

To propose a conjecture,
set

\displaystyle e_{i}

\displaystyle=e_{(i,1)}e_{(i,2)}\cdots e_{(i,r_{i})},

\displaystyle\mu_{i}

\displaystyle=\mu_{(i,1)}\mu_{(i,2)}\cdots\mu_{(i,r_{i})},

\displaystyle\nu_{i}

\displaystyle=\nu_{(i,1)}\nu_{(i,2)}\cdots\nu_{(i,r_{i})},

(87)

and
\vartheta_{i}=p_{i}\partial/\partial p_{i}

for
i\in I=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
, and

\displaystyle\Delta=\vartheta_{1}^{2}+\vartheta_{2}^{2}+\cdots+\vartheta_{N}^{2}-\vartheta_{1}\vartheta_{2}-\vartheta_{2}\vartheta_{3}-\cdots-\vartheta_{N}\vartheta_{1}.

(88)

Conjecture 4.14
.

We have

\displaystyle\left({e_{1}e_{2}\cdots e_{N}p_{1}p_{2}\cdots p_{N}\over\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\cdots\nu_{N}};\kappa^{N}\right)_{\infty}\cdot\sum_{l_{1}\geq 0}\cdots\sum_{l_{N}\geq 0}{q^{(l_{1}l_{2}+l_{2}l_{3}+\cdots+l_{N}l_{1})/2}\over(q;q)_{l_{1}}(q;q)_{l_{2}}\cdots(q;q)_{l_{N}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(-q^{1/2}e_{2}p_{1}\over\nu_{2}\right)^{l_{1}}\left(-q^{1/2}e_{3}p_{2}\over\nu_{3}\right)^{l_{2}}\cdots\left(-q^{1/2}e_{1}p_{N}\over\nu_{1}\right)^{l_{N}}

\displaystyle\cdot q^{\sum_{i\in I}(l_{i+1}-l_{i-1})\vartheta_{i}/2}q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

(89)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\cdots\mu_{N}p_{1}p_{2}\cdots p_{N}\over e_{1}e_{2}\cdots e_{N}};\kappa^{N}\right)_{\infty}\cdot\sum_{l_{1}\geq 0}\cdots\sum_{l_{N}\geq 0}{q^{(l_{1}l_{2}+l_{2}l_{3}+\cdots+l_{N}l_{1})/2}\over(q;q)_{l_{1}}(q;q)_{l_{2}}\cdots(q;q)_{l_{N}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{1}p_{1}\over e_{1}\right)^{l_{1}}\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{2}p_{2}\over e_{2}\right)^{l_{2}}\cdots\left(-q^{1/2}\mu_{N}p_{N}\over e_{N}\right)^{l_{N}}

\displaystyle\cdot q^{\sum_{i\in I}(-l_{i+1}+l_{i-1})\vartheta_{i}/2}q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa).

When
N=1
, we have

\displaystyle{Z}^{r_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}|p_{1}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1}\end{array}\Biggl|p_{1}\Biggl|q,\kappa\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{r_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}|p_{1}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\end{array}\Biggl|p_{1}\Biggl|q,\kappa\right).

Proposition 4.15
.

When
N=1
, the conjecture
4.14
is equivalent to the
following equation:

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{r_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}|p_{1}|q,\kappa).

\displaystyle={(q\kappa e_{1}p_{1}/\nu_{1};q,\kappa)_{\infty}(\mu_{1}p_{1}/e_{1};q,\kappa)_{\infty}\over(e_{1}p_{1}/\nu_{1};q,\kappa)_{\infty}(q\kappa\mu_{1}p_{1}/e_{1};q,\kappa)_{\infty}}\cdot Z^{r_{1}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}|p_{1}|q,\kappa)

(90)

Proof.

This follows from

{(A;q,\kappa)_{\infty}\over(q\kappa A;q,\kappa)_{\infty}}=(A;\kappa)_{\infty}(qA;q)_{\infty},

and the
q
-binomial theorem.
∎

For cohomology theory, we have functional equations
[
34
, (6)]

corresponding to (
90
).
Analoguous ones
[
35
, Theorem 3.3]
for quiver varieties of type
A^{(1)}_{1}
are aslo obtained.

When
N=2
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}^{-1}\end{array}\right|p_{1},p_{2}\right|q,\kappa\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}\end{array}\right|p_{2},p_{1}\right|q,\kappa\right).

Proposition 4.16
.

When
N=2
, the conjecture (
4.14
)
is equivalent
to the following equation:

\displaystyle q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle={(-q^{1/2}e_{2}p_{1}/\nu_{2};q)_{\infty}\over(-q^{1/2}\mu_{1}p_{1}/e_{1};q)_{\infty}}{(-q^{1/2}e_{1}p_{2}/\nu_{1};q)_{\infty}\over(-q^{1/2}\mu_{2}p_{2}/e_{2};q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\cdot{(q\kappa^{2}e_{1}e_{2}p_{1}p_{2}/\nu_{1}\nu_{2};q,\kappa^{2})_{\infty}(\mu_{1}\mu_{2}p_{1}p_{2}/e_{1}e_{2};q,\kappa^{2})_{\infty}\over(e_{1}e_{2}p_{1}p_{2}/\nu_{1}\nu_{2};q,\kappa^{2})_{\infty}(q\kappa^{2}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}p_{1}p_{2}/e_{1}e_{2};q,\kappa^{2})_{\infty}}q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa).

Proof.

This follows from

{(AB;q)_{\infty}\over(A;q)_{\infty}(B;q)_{\infty}}=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}{q^{mn}\over(q;q)_{m}(q;q)_{n}}A^{m}B^{n},

which is obtained by the two times
usages of
q
-binomial theorem
and
(B;q)_{m}/(B;q)_{\infty}=1/(q^{m}B;q)_{\infty}
.
∎

When
N=3
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{3}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}^{-1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{3}^{-1}\end{array}\right|p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\right|q,\kappa\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{x}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{3}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{3}\end{array}\right|p_{1},p_{3},p_{2}\right|q,\kappa\right).

When
N\geq 3
, the factor
q^{\sum_{i\in I}(l_{i+1}-l_{i-1})\vartheta_{i}/2}
in Conjecture
4.14

is non-trivial, and prevents us
from factorize
q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}/q^{-{1\over 2}\Delta}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}

unlike as in Proposition
4.15
and Proposition

4.16
.

4.8
Hilbert scheme of
points on the plane (
A^{(1)}_{0}
and
N=r=1
)

Here we study the case where

N=1
and
r_{1}=1
.
Then it is known that
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})=M^{-}(1,n)
is isomorphic to the moduli space of rank
1

framed torsion free
sheaves on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, that is, the Hilbert scheme of
n
points
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
.
We set
e=e_{1}=e_{(1,1)},\mu=\mu_{1}=\mu_{(1,1)}
,
\nu=\nu_{1}=\nu_{(1,1)}
, and
p=p_{1}
.

By Proposition
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(e,t|p|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{N}^{(0|1)}_{\lambda,\lambda}(t|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}^{(0|1)}_{\lambda,\lambda}(1|q,\kappa)}p^{|\lambda|}=\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(e,t|p|q,\kappa).

For the fundamental matter class, we have the following.

Proposition 4.17
.

When
N=1
and
r_{1}=1
, we have

\displaystyle f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}a\\
b\\
c\end{array}\right|p\right|q,\kappa\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}\nolimits\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{(1-b^{n}/c^{n})(1-a^{n}/q^{n}\kappa^{n}b^{n})\over(1-q^{-n})(1-\kappa^{-n})}\cdot{p^{n}\over n}\right).

Proof.

From the definition (
80
),
we have

\displaystyle f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}a\\
b\\
c\end{array}\right|p\right|q,\kappa\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathsf{P}}{{\mathsf{N}}^{(0|1)}_{\emptyset,\lambda}(a/b)\cdot{\mathsf{N}}^{(0|1)}_{\lambda,\emptyset}(b/c)\over\mathsf{N}_{\lambda,\lambda}^{(0|1)}(1|q,\kappa)}p^{|\lambda|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathsf{P}}\prod_{s=(l,m)\in Y_{\lambda}}{(1-a\kappa^{-l}q^{-m}/b)(1-b\kappa^{l-1}q^{m-1}b/c)\over(1-\kappa^{L_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)}q^{-A_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)-1})(1-\kappa^{-L_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)-1}q^{A_{Y_{\lambda}}(s)})}.

Then we get the assertion by substituting
(q,t)=(q^{-1},\kappa^{-1})
,

X=(1-b/c),Y=(1-a/q\kappa b)
into
[
29
, (1.7)]

under the principal specialization
[
27
, (6.17)]

of the Macdonald polynomial.
∎

By Proposition
4.13
and Proposition
4.17
,
we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(e,\mu,\nu|p|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\nu^{-1}\\
e^{-1}\\
\mu^{-1}\end{array}\right|p\right|q,\kappa\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}\nolimits\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{(1-e^{n}/q^{n}\kappa^{n}\nu^{n})(1-\mu^{n}/e^{n})\over(1-q^{-n})(1-\kappa^{-n})}\cdot{p^{n}\over n}\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(e,\mu,\nu|p|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\mu\\
e\\
\nu\end{array}\right|p\right|q,\kappa\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}\nolimits\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{(1-e^{n}/\nu^{n})(1-\mu^{n}/q^{n}\kappa^{n}e^{n})\over(1-q^{-n})(1-\kappa^{-n})}\cdot{p^{n}\over n}\right).

Then Conjecture
4.14

follows from
Proposition
4.15
, since
we have

\displaystyle{\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(e,\mu,\nu|p|q,\kappa)\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(e,\mu,\nu|p|q,\kappa)}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}\nolimits\left(-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\kappa^{n}q^{n}e^{n}/\nu^{n}-e^{n}/\nu^{n}-\kappa^{n}q^{n}\mu^{n}/e^{n}-\mu^{n}/e^{n}\over(1-q^{n})(1-\kappa^{n})}\cdot{p^{n}\over n}\right)

\displaystyle={(q\kappa ep/\nu;q,\kappa)_{\infty}(\mu p/e;q,\kappa)_{\infty}\over(ep/\nu;q,\kappa)_{\infty}(q\kappa\mu p/e;q,\kappa)_{\infty}}.

4.9
A_{N-1}
Limit

When
v_{i}=0
for some
i\in I
,
the framed quiver moduli space

M^{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
is called the

handsaw quiver variety
of type
A_{N-1}
.
In particular,
M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
with
v_{i}=0

is called the
Laumon space
.
Generating series of integrals of
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
and

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}
over Laumon spaces with the fixed framing

\boldsymbol{r}
are obtained by substituting
p_{i}=0
into

Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},t|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)
and

Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)
.
Here we consider the case where
v_{N}=0
, and hence

p_{N}=0
for simplicity.

For variables
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{i})_{i\in I}
and

\boldsymbol{s}=(\boldsymbol{s}_{\alpha})_{\alpha=1}^{d}
with

\boldsymbol{s}_{\alpha}=(s_{(i,\alpha)})_{i\in I}
for

\alpha=1,\ldots,r_{i}
, set

\displaystyle f^{\mathfrak{gl}_{N}}(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{s}|q,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\theta\in(M_{N})^{d}}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}c_{N}(\theta^{(\alpha)},\theta^{(\beta)}|\boldsymbol{s}_{\alpha},\boldsymbol{s}_{\beta}|q,t)\prod_{1\leq i<k\leq N}(x_{k}/x_{i})^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}},

where
M_{N}
is the set of
N\times N
strictly upper
triangular matrices with non-negative integer entries,
and for
\theta=(\theta^{(\alpha)})_{\alpha=1}^{d}\in(M_{N})^{d}
with
\theta^{(\alpha)}=(\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,j})_{i,j\in I}\in M_{N}
,
we set

\displaystyle c_{N}(\theta^{(\alpha)},\theta^{(\beta)}|\boldsymbol{s}_{\alpha},\boldsymbol{s}_{\beta}|q,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq k\leq N}{(q^{\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}ts_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}\over(q^{\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}qs_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{1\leq i\leq j<k\leq N}\frac{(q^{-\theta^{(\beta)}_{jk}+\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}qs_{(j,\beta)}/ts_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}}{(q^{-\theta^{(\beta)}_{jk}+\sum_{a>k}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ia}-\theta^{(\beta)}_{ja}}s_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{ik}}}.

Proposition 4.18
.

(a)
When
r_{1}=\cdots=r_{N}=d
, we have

Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(\kappa^{\delta}\boldsymbol{s},q/t|x_{2}/tx_{1},\ldots,x_{N}/tx_{N-1},0|q,\kappa)=f^{\mathfrak{gl}_{N}}\left(\left.\left.\boldsymbol{x}\right|\boldsymbol{s}\right|q,t\right).

(b)
We have

\left.Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|\boldsymbol{p}|q,\kappa)\right|_{x_{N}=0}=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\boldsymbol{0}&\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}^{-1}&\ldots&\boldsymbol{\nu}_{N-1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{\nu}_{N}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}&\ldots&\boldsymbol{e}_{N-1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{N}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}^{-1}&\ldots&\boldsymbol{\mu}_{N-1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{0}\end{array}\right|p_{1},\ldots,p_{N-1},0\right|q,\kappa\right).

Proof.

(a) By
[
9
]
and
[
24
, Corollary 3.4]
, we have

\lim_{p\to 0}f^{\hat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{N}}(p^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{x},p^{1/N}|\kappa^{\delta/N}\boldsymbol{s},\kappa^{1/N}|q,q/t)=f^{\mathfrak{gl}_{N}}\left(\left.\left.\boldsymbol{x}\right|\boldsymbol{s}\right|q,t\right).

Hence the assertion follows from (
73
)
substituting
p^{1/N}=p
and
\kappa^{1/N}=\kappa

since
p
and
\kappa
vanish in the right hand side.
Here we give a direct proof for the convenience.
Taking
p_{N}=0
, the partitions producing non-vanishing
contribution to the summation satisfy

\ell(\lambda^{(i,\alpha)})\leq N-i
for

i=1,\ldots,N
and
1\leq\alpha\leq d
.
Hence we can parametrize them by using the set

(M_{N})^{d}
as

\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k}=\sum_{j=i+k}^{N}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,j}
.
Namely
\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,j}=\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j-i}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{j-i+1}
.
Then for
1\leq i\leq j\leq N
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(u|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq l\leq l^{\prime}\leq N-i\atop l^{\prime}=l+j-i}(uq^{-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{l}+\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{l^{\prime}+1}}\kappa^{-l+l^{\prime}};q)_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{l^{\prime}}-\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{l^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=j+1}^{N}(uq^{-\sum_{a=k}^{N}\theta^{(\beta)}_{j,a}+\sum_{a=k+1}^{N}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,a}}\kappa^{j-i};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,k}}

setting
k=l+j
.
For
1\leq j<i\leq N
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(u|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq l\leq l^{\prime}\leq N-j\atop l^{\prime}=l+i-j-1}(uq^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{l}-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{l^{\prime}}}\kappa^{l-l^{\prime}-1};q)_{\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{l^{\prime}}-\lambda^{(j,\beta)}_{l^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=i}^{N}(uq^{\sum_{a=k+1}^{N}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,a}-\sum_{a=k}^{N}\theta^{(\beta)}_{j,a}}\kappa^{j-i};q)_{\theta^{(\beta)}_{j,k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=i}^{N}A^{\theta^{(\beta)}_{jk}}q^{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,k}(\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,k}-1)/2}(uq^{\sum_{a=k+1}^{N}\theta^{(\beta)}_{j,a}-\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,a}}q\kappa^{i-j};q)_{\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,k}}

setting
k=i+l-1
and

A=uq^{\sum_{a=k+1}^{N}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,a}-\sum_{a=k}^{N}\theta^{(\beta)}_{j,a}}\kappa^{j-i}
.
Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}\prod_{i,j\in I}{{\mathsf{N}}^{(j-i|N)}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}(\kappa^{i-j}qs_{(j,\beta)}/ts_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa)\over\mathsf{N}_{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)},\lambda^{(j,\beta)}}^{(j-i|N)}(\kappa^{i-j}s_{(j,\beta)}/s_{(i,\alpha)}|q,\kappa)}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{d}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{k=1}^{N-i}(x_{i+k}/tx_{i+k-1})^{\lambda^{(i,\alpha)}_{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{d}c_{N}(\theta^{(\alpha)},\theta^{(\beta)}|s_{\alpha},s_{\beta}|q,t)\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq N}(x_{j}/x_{i})^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d}\theta^{(\alpha)}_{i,j}}.

(b) This directly follows from the definition
(
76
).
∎

When
N=2
, by Proposition
4.18
(b),
we have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|p_{1},0|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1}&0\end{array}\right|p_{1},0\right|q,1\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|p_{1},0|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{cc}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1}&0\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}\\
0&\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}^{-1}\end{array}\right|p_{1},0\right|q,1\right),

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|0,p_{2}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{cc}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}^{-1}&0\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}\\
0&\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}^{-1}\end{array}\right|0,p_{2}\right|q,1\right),

\displaystyle\widecheck{Z}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|0,p_{2}|q,\kappa)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left.\left.\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{-1}\\
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}^{-1}&0\end{array}\right|0,p_{2}\right|q,1\right).

5
Handsaw quiver variety of type
A_{1}

In the rest of the paper,
we consider the case
N=2
and
v_{1}=0
.
We use the indices
0,1
in
I=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
instead of
1,2

as in the previous section.
We set
M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
,

M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=M^{\mathop{\mathrm{AL}}\nolimits^{\vee}}(\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{v})
for

\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{0},r_{1})
and
\boldsymbol{v}=(v_{0},v_{1})=(n,0)
.
They are called the
handsaw quiver varieties

of type
A_{1}
.

In other words, these spaces are regarded as framed
quiver moduli for the framed quiver
Q\colon

where we have only one vertex
\ast
other than
\infty
,
and
r_{0}
framings and
r_{1}
co-framings.
Hence a dimension vector
\alpha=(1,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}

are prescribed by a non-negative integer
n
.
We choose stability parameters
\zeta^{-}<0<\zeta^{+}
so that we have
M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=M_{Q}^{\zeta^{\pm}}(\alpha)
.

Furthermore we use

J_{0}=\{1,\ldots,r_{0}\}
,
J_{1}=\{r_{0}+1,\ldots,r=r_{0}+r_{1}\}
,
and set

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}e_{(0,\alpha)}&\alpha\in J_{0}\\
e_{(1,\alpha-r_{0})}&\alpha\in J_{1}\end{cases}

(124)

so that we have

W_{0}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},W_{1}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}}
.

We set
V=V_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
\kappa=1
.
Then in (
50
), we have
\mathbb{L}=0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}(W,V)=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(W_{0},V)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,W_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q},

and get the explicit descriptions

M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=[\mathbb{M}^{\pm}/\operatorname{GL}(V)]
and

\mathcal{V}=[\mathbb{M}^{\pm}\times V/\operatorname{GL}(V)]
,
where

\mathbb{M}^{\pm}
is the
\zeta^{\pm}
-stable locus in

\mathbb{M}
.
Set

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}q/\mu_{(0,\alpha)}&\alpha\in J_{0}\\
1/\nu_{(1,\alpha-r_{0})}&\alpha\in J_{1}.\end{cases}

(125)

Then in (
72
) and (
76
), we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{V})+\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}+\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{V})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}-\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{V}),

(126)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mu_{\alpha}/q}+\bigoplus_{\alpha=r_{0}+1}^{r}\mathcal{V}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q/\mu_{\alpha}},

(127)

where
\mathcal{W}_{0}=\mathcal{O}_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\otimes W_{0}
and

\mathcal{W}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\otimes W_{1}
.
We set

Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p^{n}\int^{[K]}_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}),\quad Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p^{n}\int^{[K]}_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}).

We have

Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},t|p,0|q,1)
,

Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)=Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},t|p,0|q,1)
by Proposition

4.4
, and

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)

\displaystyle=\widecheck{Z}_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|p,0|q,1),

\displaystyle Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}|p,0|q,1)

by (
4.9
), (
4.9
).
Here we substitute

\boldsymbol{e}=((e_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in J_{0}},(e_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in J_{1}}),\boldsymbol{\mu}=((q/\mu_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in J_{0}},(0)_{\alpha\in J_{1}}),\boldsymbol{\nu}=((0)_{\alpha\in J_{0}},(1/\mu_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in J_{1}}).

5.1
Combinatorial description

The fixed points set
M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)^{\mathbb{T}}

can be identified with

\{\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}\mid|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\}
,
where we regard
(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}
as a subset of
(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}
and set

|\boldsymbol{k}|=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r}k_{\alpha}
.
The
\mathbb{T}
-fixed point in
M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)
corresponding to
\boldsymbol{k}
is described by

\displaystyle V=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{-i+1}},\quad W_{0}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},\quad W_{1}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},

(128)

B(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{-i+1}})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{-i}}&\text{ if }1\leq i<k_{\alpha}\\
0&\text{ if }i=k_{\alpha}\\
\end{cases},\quad z(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}})=\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},

and
w=0
.
Hence the tangent space
T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)

at the point corresponding to
\boldsymbol{k}
is

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,V)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}+\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(W_{0},V)+\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,W_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}-\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,V)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in J_{0}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{\beta}}(q^{j-i+1}-q^{j-i})+\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}q^{-i+1}\right)+\sum_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{0}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{i}.

(129)

Here we have calculated (
128
) and (
129
) in

K_{\mathbb{T}}(\text{pt})=\mathbb{Z}[q^{\pm 1},t^{\pm 1},\boldsymbol{e}^{\pm 1},\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\pm 1}]
where

q,t,\boldsymbol{e}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r})
and

\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{r})
are equivariant parameters.

Similarly, we can identify
M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)^{\mathbb{T}}
with

\{\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{1}}\mid|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\}.

The
\mathbb{T}
-fixed point in
M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)
corresponding to
\boldsymbol{k}
is described by

\displaystyle V=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{i}},\quad W_{0}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},\quad W_{1}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}},

(130)

B(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{i}})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{i-1}}&\text{ if }i>1\\
0&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases},\quad w(\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}q^{i}})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }i>1\\
\mathbb{C}_{e_{\alpha}}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases},

and
z=0
.
The tangent space
T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)

at the point corresponding to
\boldsymbol{k}
is

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,V)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}+\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(W_{0},V)+\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,W_{1})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{q}-\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{C}}(V,V)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{\beta}}(q^{i-j+1}-q^{i-j})+\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{-i+1}\right)+\sum_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{0}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\alpha}}q^{i}.

(131)

Hence we have

\displaystyle T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)=T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n))|_{e_{1}=e_{1}^{-1},\ldots,e_{r}=e_{r}^{-1}}.

(132)

5.2
Adjoint matter class
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}

We take
K
-theory classes (

)
called

after physics theory.

Proposition 5.1
.

We have

\displaystyle T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}^{-1}e_{\beta}\sum_{\ell=k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}+1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{\ell}+\sum_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}^{-1}e_{\beta}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{i},

(133)

\displaystyle T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in J_{0}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\sum_{\ell=k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}+1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{\ell}+\sum_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{0}}e_{\alpha}e_{\beta}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\beta}}q^{i}.

(134)

Proof.

It follows from (

) and (

).
See
[

, Proposition 2.2]
.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

For
\boldsymbol{k}\in M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)^{\mathbb{T}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n))}{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n))}

\displaystyle=t^{nr_{0}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
