\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\beta}/te_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

(135)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n))}{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(T_{\boldsymbol{k}}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n))}

\displaystyle=t^{nr_{1}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(tq^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\alpha}/te_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

(136)

where
(x;q)_{k}=(1-x)(1-qx)\cdots(1-q^{k-1}x)

is the
q
-Pochhammer symbol.

We rewrite Proposition
5.2
in the form of generating series.

Theorem 5.3
.

We have

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\left(t^{r_{0}}p\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(tq^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{1}\\
\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\beta}/te_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}},

\displaystyle Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{r_{0}}\end{subarray}}\left(t^{r_{1}}p\right)^{\lvert\boldsymbol{k}\rvert}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(tq^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(qe_{\alpha}/te_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}.

By applying Theorem
3.8
, we give a proof of the
following theorem in the next section.

Theorem 5.4
.

We have

\displaystyle Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p)

\displaystyle=\frac{(qt^{r_{0}}p;q,t)_{\infty}(t^{r_{1}+1}p;q,t)_{\infty}}{(t^{r_{0}+1}p;q,t)_{\infty}(qt^{r_{1}}p;q,t)_{\infty}}\cdot Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e};p).

(137)

When
(r_{0},r_{1})=(1,0)
we have

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}H_{n}^{+}p^{n}=1
and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}H_{n}^{-}p^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(tq^{-n})_{n}}{(q^{-n})_{n}}p^{n}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(q/t)_{n}}{(q)_{n}}(tp)^{n}=\frac{(qp)_{\infty}}{(tp)_{\infty}}.

When
(r_{0},r_{1})=(0,1)
we have

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}H_{n}^{-}p^{n}=1
and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}H_{n}^{-}p^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(tq^{-n})_{n}}{(q^{-n})_{n}}p^{n}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(q/t)_{n}}{(q)_{n}}(tp)^{n}=\frac{(qp)_{\infty}}{(tp)_{\infty}}.

In these cases, we can easily check (
137
).

Lemma 5.5
.

When
r_{0}=1
, the set

\left\{H_{n}\right\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{C}(q,t)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r_{1}})
is
linearly independent over
\mathbb{C}(q,t)
, where

x_{i}=e_{i+1}/e_{1}
for
i=1,\ldots,r_{1}
.

Proof.

Up to scalar,
H_{n}
is equal to
f_{n}(x;a,b)=\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\frac{(ax_{i})_{n}}{(bx_{i})_{n}}

with
a=q/e
and
b=q
by Proposition
5.1
.
It is enough to show
\{H_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
is linearly independent over
\mathbb{C}(q,t)(x_{2},\ldots,x_{r_{1}})
.
Hence we may assume
r_{1}=1
.
Then we can show that
\sum_{k=0}^{n}c_{k}f_{k}(x;a,b)=0
implies
c_{k}=0
for
k=0,1,\ldots,n
as follows.

For
q
-difference operator
T_{q,x}
, we have
(T_{q,x}-1)f_{k}(x;a,b)=\frac{x(a-b)(1-q^{k})}{(bx;q)_{2}}f_{k-1}(x;aq,bq^{2})
.
Hence if we set
\mathcal{D}^{(\ell)}=\mathcal{D}_{q^{\ell-1}a,q^{2(\ell-1)}b}\cdots\mathcal{D}_{qa,q^{2}b}\mathcal{D}_{a,b}
with

\mathcal{D}_{a,b}=\frac{(bx;q)_{2}}{x(a-b)}\left(T_{q,x}-1\right),

then we have
\mathcal{D}^{(\ell)}f_{k}(x;a,b)\Big|_{x=1/aq^{\ell}}=(1;q)_{k}\cdot\delta_{k\ell}
.
This gives the assertion.
∎

5.3
Fundamental matter class
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}

We take
K
-theory classes (
127
)
called
fundamental matter classes
after physics theory.
Set
I_{n}^{\pm}=\int^{[K]}_{M^{\pm}(\alpha)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})
,
and
Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}I_{n}^{\pm}p^{n}
.
By (
128
), (
130
) and Proposition
5.2
,
we have

\displaystyle I^{+}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{1}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}+1}/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}\over(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}\over(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}

(138)

\displaystyle I^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}\over(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}\over(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}

(139)

Lemma 5.6
.

We have

\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(q^{-k_{\beta}}x_{\alpha}/x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}=(-1)^{|\boldsymbol{k}|}q^{\frac{|\boldsymbol{k}|(|\boldsymbol{k}|+1)}{2}}\prod_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq m}\frac{q^{k_{\alpha}}x_{\alpha}-q^{k_{\beta}}x_{\beta}}{x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(qx_{\alpha}/x_{\beta};q)_{k_{\alpha}}}

Proof.

For
1\leq\alpha=\beta\leq m
,
the corresponding factor in the left hand side is equal to

(q^{-k_{\beta}})_{k_{\beta}}=(-1)^{k_{\beta}}q^{\frac{k_{\beta}(k_{\beta}+1)}{2}}(q)_{k_{\beta}}.

For
\alpha\neq\beta
, we have

\displaystyle(q^{-k_{\beta}}e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\alpha}}\cdot(q^{-k_{\alpha}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{beta}}

\displaystyle=q^{-\frac{k_{\alpha}(2k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}+1)}{2}}(-e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})^{k_{\alpha}}(q^{k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}+1}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\alpha}}(q^{-k_{\alpha}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}

\displaystyle=q^{-\frac{k_{\alpha}(2k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}+1)}{2}}(-e_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})^{k_{\alpha}}\frac{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}(1-e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})(q^{-k_{\alpha}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\alpha}}}{1-q^{k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=q^{-k_{\alpha}-k_{\beta}k_{\alpha}}\frac{1-e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha}}{1-q^{k_{\beta}-k_{\alpha}}e_{\beta}/e_{\alpha}}(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=q^{-k_{\beta}k_{\alpha}}\frac{e_{\alpha}-e_{\beta}}{q^{k_{\alpha}}e_{\alpha}-q^{k_{\beta}}e_{\beta}}(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\alpha}}.

Hence we get the assertion.
∎

Using this lemma, we rewrite (
138
) and (
139
).

Proposition 5.7
.

For
\boldsymbol{k}\in M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)^{\mathbb{T}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\wedge_{-1}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})^{\vee}_{\boldsymbol{k}}}{\wedge_{-1}T_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\ast}M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}

\displaystyle=\left(-q^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\prod_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\frac{q}{e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{1}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e})}{\Delta_{J_{1}}(\boldsymbol{e})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha};q)_{k_{\beta}}}

(140)

\displaystyle\frac{\wedge_{-1}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})^{\vee}_{\boldsymbol{k}}}{\wedge_{-1}T_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\ast}M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}

\displaystyle=\left(-q^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\prod_{\alpha\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{0}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}{\Delta_{J_{0}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}

(141)

where
\Delta_{K}(x)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in K\\
\alpha<\beta\end{subarray}}(x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta})

for any subset
K\subset[r]
.

Proof.

We compare (
138
) and (
140
).
In the first product, we get the coefficient

(q/e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha})^{k_{\beta}}

corresponding to
\alpha\in J_{0},\beta\in J_{1}
.
Applying Lemma
5.6
to the second product,
we get (
140
).
Similarly we compare (
139
) and (
141
), and
use Lemma
5.6
to get the assertion.
∎

We summarize a result of generating series

Z_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\mu,p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}I_{n}^{\pm}p^{n}
.

Theorem 5.8
.

We have

\displaystyle Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{1}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(-pq^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\prod_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\frac{q}{e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{1}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e})}{\Delta_{J_{1}}(\boldsymbol{e})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{1}\\
\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}},

\displaystyle Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}^{\boldsymbol{r}}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu},p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(-pq^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\prod_{\alpha\in J_{1}}e_{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{0}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}{\Delta_{J_{0}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}.

5.4
Borel transformation

We set
\hat{I}^{\pm}_{n}=q^{-n(n+1)/2}I^{\pm}_{n}

where

I^{+}_{n}=\int^{[K]}_{M^{+}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}C(\mathcal{V}),\quad I^{-}_{n}=\int^{[K]}_{M^{-}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}C(\mathcal{V}).

These are coefficients of

\mathcal{B}^{-1}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\pm\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}(q,\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{\mu};p)=\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}I^{\pm}_{n}p^{n}\right)
.
Here
\mathcal{B}
is the
q
-Borel transformation acting on

p
-series defined by

\mathcal{B}\cdot p^{n}=q^{n(n+1)/2}p^{n}
for

n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

By Proposition
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{+}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{1}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{-1}{e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}}/q^{r_{0}}}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{1}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e})}{\Delta_{J_{1}}(\boldsymbol{e})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{1}\\
\beta\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{1}\\
\alpha\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\beta}/e_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}

(142)

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{k}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{J_{0}}\\
|\boldsymbol{k}|=n\end{subarray}}\left(-e_{r_{0}+1}\mu_{r_{0}+1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r}\right)^{n}\frac{\Delta_{J_{0}}(q^{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}{\Delta_{J_{0}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{-1})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in J_{0}\\
\beta\in J_{0}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in J_{0}\\
\alpha\in J_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{(q/e_{\beta}\mu_{\alpha})_{k_{\beta}}}{(qe_{\alpha}/e_{\beta})_{k_{\beta}}}.

(143)

Theorem 5.9
.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{I}^{+}_{n}p^{n}={(-pe_{r_{0}+1}\mu_{r_{0}+1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r};q)_{\infty}\over(-q^{r_{0}}p/e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}};q)_{\infty}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{I}^{-}_{n}p^{n}.

(144)

This is equivalent to the Kajihara transformation formula
(
1.3
) given in
[
20
, Theorem 1.1]

as we remarked after Theorem
1.10
.
In the next section,
we give a proof using wall-crossing formula
(
41
).

Comparing
\mathcal{B}
with
q^{-{\Delta\over 2}}
,
we substitute
p=q^{1/2}p
in (
144
) to get
Proposition
4.16
under
p_{1}=0,p_{2}=p

after the changes
(
124
) and (
125
) of variables.

6
Wall-crossing formula for handsaw quiver variety

We consider one of
K
-theory classes (
126
) and
(
127
).
Using Lemma
3.6
, we deduce explicit
transformation formula.

We have
H_{Q}(d^{\sharp})\cong M((1,0),d^{\sharp})
.
To apply results in §
2
and §
3
to the handsaw quiver variety,
we need lemmas.

Lemma 6.1
.

We have an isomorphism
M^{-}((1,0),d^{\sharp})\cong\mathbb{A}^{d^{\sharp}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\sharp}}]

such that the tautological bundle
\mathcal{V}^{\sharp}

corresponds to

\mathbb{C}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\sharp}}]
-module

\mathbb{C}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\sharp}},y]/(y^{d^{\sharp}}+x_{1}y^{d^{\sharp}-1}+\cdots+x_{d^{\sharp}})

via this isomorphism.
Furthermore
q\in T
acts by
q^{-1}y
and

q^{i}x_{i}
for

i=1,\ldots,d^{\sharp}
.

6.1
Adjoint matter theory

By Lemma
6.1
, we have

\gamma_{d}(t)=t^{d}{(q/t)_{d}\over(q)_{d}}
.
We set

a_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\boldsymbol{r})=\prod_{i=1}^{j}{t^{d^{(i)}}[d^{(i)}-1]_{t}!\over t-1}{(q/t)_{d^{(i)}}\over(q)_{d^{(i)}}}\left(t^{s(\mathfrak{I}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i})+r_{1}d^{(i)}-r_{0}d^{(i)}}-t^{s(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}^{>i},\mathfrak{I}^{(i)})}\right).

For
H^{\pm}_{n}=\int_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits})
, we have

\displaystyle H^{+}_{n}-H^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}_{j}^{n}}{t^{r_{0}|\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}|}\cdot}{[n-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert]_{t}!\over[n]_{t}!}a_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\boldsymbol{r})H^{-}_{n-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert}

(145)

by Theorem
3.8
.

Lemma 6.2
.

When
r_{0}=r_{1}
, we have

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{Dec}_{j}^{n}\\
\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert=k\end{subarray}}{[n-\lvert\boldsymbol{d}_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}\rvert]_{t}!\over[n]_{t}!}a_{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{I}}}(\boldsymbol{r})=0

Proof.

When
r_{0}=r_{1}
, the left hand side does not depend
on
(r_{0},r_{1})
.
Hence, we can reduce computations to the case where

r_{0}=r_{1}=1
.
In this case, we see that
H_{n}^{+}-H_{n}^{-}=0
in
(
145
).
Since
H^{-}_{0},\ldots,H^{-}_{n-1}
is linearly
independent by Lemma
5.5
.
This gives the assertion.
∎

By this lemma, we have
Z_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{(r_{0},r_{1})}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e},p)=Z_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}^{(r_{0},r_{1})}(q,t,\boldsymbol{e},p)
when
r_{0}=r_{1}
.
From this, we deduce (
137
) by
Theorem
1.9
.
In fact, substituting
\tau=q/t
,

x_{\alpha}=e_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in J_{1}
, and

y_{\alpha}=q/te_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in J_{0}

in (
8
), we get (
3
) and
(
4
).
When
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
, we have

\prod_{s=1}^{r_{1}-r_{0}}{(qt^{r_{0}+s-1}p;q)_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{0}+s}p;q)_{\infty}}={(qt^{r_{0}}p;q,t)_{\infty}(t^{r_{1}+1}p;q,t)_{\infty}\over(t^{r_{0}+1}p;q)_{\infty}(qt^{r_{1}}p;q,t)_{\infty}}
,
and the transformation formula
(
8
) in Theorem
1.9

is equivalent to

\displaystyle{Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}\over Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}}=\prod_{s=1}^{r_{1}-r_{0}}\frac{(qt^{r_{0}+s-1}p;q)_{\infty}}{(t^{r_{0}+s}p;q)_{\infty}}

(146)

When
r_{0}\geq r_{1}
, we can replace the role of
x

and
y
to get the similar formula in
Theorem
1.9
.
Then we have

Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{-\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}=\prod_{s=1}^{r_{0}-r_{1}}\frac{(qt^{r_{1}+s-1}p;q)_{\infty}}{(t^{r_{1}+s}p;q)_{\infty}}Z^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{+\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}\nolimits}
.
This also implies (
146
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
5.4
.

6.2
Fundamental matter theory

We take
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits}
in (
127
).
We set

F(\mathcal{V})=\prod_{\alpha\in J_{0}}\wedge_{-q/\mu_{\alpha}}\mathcal{V}^{\vee}\cdot\prod_{\alpha\in J_{1}}\wedge_{-\mu_{\alpha}/q}\mathcal{V}.

Then we have
\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits F(\mathcal{V})=\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}^{t}(\Lambda_{fund})
.
For
\boldsymbol{d}=(d^{(1)},\ldots,d^{(j)})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{j}
, we compute
C_{\boldsymbol{d}}
in
(
42
) part by part.
By Lemma
2.14
, we have

\displaystyle\oint d\hbar_{i}\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\mu_{\alpha}/q+\sum_{\alpha=r_{0}+1}^{r}\mathcal{V}^{(i)\vee}\otimes e^{-\hbar_{i}}q/\mu_{\alpha}\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}\left(\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}},\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{l=i+1}^{j}\mathcal{V}^{(l)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{l}})\right).}

\displaystyle=\oint d\hbar_{i}{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\wedge_{-1}\left(\sum_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{V}^{(i)\vee}\otimes e^{-\hbar_{i}}q/\mu_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha=r_{0}+1}^{r}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{i}}\mu_{\alpha}/q\right)\over\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\wedge_{-1}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)\vee}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{0}e^{-\hbar_{i}}+\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\vee}e^{\hbar_{i}}/q\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot{\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\wedge_{-1}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{>i}e^{-\hbar_{i}}+\mathcal{V}^{>i\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{(i)}e^{\hbar_{i}}\right)\over\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\wedge_{-1}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{>i}e^{-\hbar_{i}}/q+\mathcal{V}^{>i\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{(i)}e^{\hbar_{i}}/q\right)}

\displaystyle={q^{r_{0}d^{(i)}+(n-|\boldsymbol{d}|_{i})d^{(i)}}\over(e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}})^{d^{(i)}}}\oint d\hbar_{i}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{r_{0}}{(e^{\hbar_{i}}q^{-d^{(i)}}\mu_{\alpha})_{d^{(i)}}\over(e^{\hbar_{i}}q^{-d^{(i)}+1}/e_{\alpha})_{d^{(i)}}}\prod_{\alpha=r_{0}+1}^{r}{(e^{\hbar_{i}}q^{-d^{(i)}}\mu_{\alpha})_{d^{(i)}}\over(e^{\hbar_{i}}q^{-d^{(i)}}/e_{\alpha})_{d^{(i)}}}

\displaystyle={q^{(n-|\boldsymbol{d}|_{i})d^{(i)}}\over(e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}}/q^{r_{0}})^{d^{(i)}}}((e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r}/q^{r_{0}})^{d^{(i)}}-1)

(147)

where we set
\mathcal{V}^{>i}=\mathcal{V}\oplus\bigoplus_{k=i+1}^{j}\mathcal{V}^{(k)}\otimes e^{\hbar_{k}}
and
|\boldsymbol{d}|_{i}=d^{(1)}+\cdots+d^{(i)}
for
i=0,\ldots,j
.
Furthermore we have
H_{Q}(d^{(i)})=M^{-}((1,0),d^{(i)})
, and

\displaystyle\int^{[K]}_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)})}\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}\nolimits\wedge_{-1}\left(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}/\mathcal{O}_{H_{Q}(d^{(i)})}\right)^{\vee}

\displaystyle={(q)_{d^{(i)}-1}\over(q^{-d^{(i)}})_{d^{(i)}}}={(-1)^{d^{(i)}}q^{d^{(i)}(d^{(i)}+1)/2}\over 1-q^{d^{(i)}}}.

(148)

For
I_{n}=\int_{M^{\pm}(\boldsymbol{r},n)}\mathop{\mathrm{Eu}}\nolimits_{K}(\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{fund}}\nolimits})
, we have

\displaystyle I^{+}_{n}-I^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}(-1)^{j}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{j}\atop|\boldsymbol{d}|\leq n}}\prod_{i=1}^{j}{(-1)^{d^{(i)}}\over|\boldsymbol{d}|_{i}}{q^{(n-|\boldsymbol{d}|_{i})d^{(i)}+d^{(i)}(d^{(i)}+1)/2}\over 1-q^{d^{(i)}}}{(e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r}/q^{r_{0}})^{d^{(i)}}-1\over(e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}}/q^{r_{0}})^{d^{(i)}}}I^{-}_{n-|\boldsymbol{d}|}

(149)

by (
43
).
For
\hat{I}^{\pm}_{n}=q^{-n(n+1)/2}\cdot I^{\pm}_{n}
,
we can rewrite (
149
) as follows :

\displaystyle\hat{I}^{+}_{n}-\hat{I}^{-}_{n}

\displaystyle=q^{-n(n+1)/2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}q^{(n-l)(n-l+1)/2}\hat{I}^{-}_{n-l}

\displaystyle\cdot(-1)^{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0=c_{0}<c_{1}<\cdots<c_{j}=l\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j}\frac{1}{c_{i}}\cdot\frac{q^{(n-c_{i})(c_{i}-c_{i-1})+(c_{i}-c_{i-1})(c_{i}-c_{i-1}+1)/2}}{1-q^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}}\left(B^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}-A^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\hat{I}^{-}_{n-l}\cdot(-B)^{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0=c_{0}<c_{1}<\cdots<c_{j}=l\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j}\frac{1}{c_{i}}\cdot\frac{1-(A/B)^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}}{1-q^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}},

(150)

where
B=q^{r_{0}}/e_{1}\mu_{1}\cdots e_{r_{0}}\mu_{r_{0}}
,
and
A=e_{r_{0}+1}\mu_{r_{0}+1}\cdots e_{r}\mu_{r}
.

In (
150
), the coefficients on
\hat{I}_{n-l}

does not depend on
n
nor
r
,
hence it is reduced to the case where
r_{0}=1
and
r_{1}=0
.
In this case, we see that
\sum_{n=0}\hat{I}^{+}_{n}p^{n}=1
and

\sum_{n=0}\hat{I}^{-}_{n}p^{n}=(-qp/e_{1}\mu_{1})_{\infty}/(-p)_{\infty}

by (
143
).
Hence we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0=c_{0}<c_{1}<\cdots<c_{j}=l\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j}\frac{1}{c_{i}}\cdot\frac{1-(1/B)^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}}{1-q^{c_{i}-c_{i-1}}}=\frac{(1/B)_{l}}{(q)_{l}}.

Substituting
B=B/A
, we have

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{I}^{+}_{n}p^{n}={(-pA;q)_{\infty}\over(-pB;q)_{\infty}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\hat{I}^{-}_{n}p^{n}.

This gives a proof of (
144
).
